P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

B5278th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: price adds about +2% to group revenue in 2027 (about SEK 0.8bn gross, about SEK 0.7bn after a partial LPB and cartonboard give-back), and about +1% a year from 2028 to 2030. But roughly half of the 2027 price gain only offsets non-wood input inflation (about SEK 0.4bn), so price's net contribution to the EBITDA margin in 2027 is about +0.7 to +0.8 points. From 2028 price is margin-neutral: it moves with cost, not ahead of it. Real pricing power exists in about 40% of sales (kraftliner, sack, semichemical fluting, specialty kraft) and behind US tariffs (about 22%), but is absent in cartonboard and weakly held in LPB, where annual price resets now transmit the 2026 wood relief to customers.

The evidence on 2026 price, by quarter. Q2 2026 net sales -4% year on year: price -2%, currency -2%, volume and mix flat. Q3 2026 +3%: volume +4%, price -1%, mix and currency flat (company presentations). Sequentially, Europe rose in Q3 (fluting, liner, brown sack and kraft) and management guides about +1% in both regions for Q4 2026, with positive momentum into H1 2027. So year-on-year price turns positive only in Q4 2026.

My price index (group, 2026 average = 100). Q1 2026 99.5, Q2 99.5, Q3 100, Q4 101; Q1 2027 102, Q2 102.5, Q3 102.5, Q4 102. The 2027 average is +2.25% on 2026. On about SEK 37bn of product sales that is about SEK 0.83bn gross; I deduct about SEK 0.1bn for the January 2027 LPB and cartonboard rounds, leaving about SEK 0.7bn to 0.75bn.

By grade (shares of sales from the first set).

  • Containerboard, fluting and kraftliner (about 13%), sack (9%), kraft and specialty (16%): real price, 2027 positive, narrowing from H2 2027. Unbleached kraftliner is up EUR 200 a tonne from its Q1 2026 low; its premium over recycled testliner is EUR 230 a tonne against a ten-year average of EUR 80 (Jefferies, 16 October 2026). Mondi secured EUR 50 a tonne for unbleached sack kraft into Q4 2026, but Mondi's own contracts take several quarters, so the full benefit reaches the P&L by Q2 2027 (Morgan Stanley, 8 October 2026). US kraftliner exports to Europe fell from above 50kt to about 29kt a month. Against this: Palm King's Lynn (700kt, March 2027) and Eren Shotton (750kt, 2027); sell-side analysts expect the September 2026 recycled increases of EUR 60 to 80 a tonne to be given back by Q2 2027 if gas eases. My judgement: the premium narrows from H2 2027, but the virgin price falls less than testliner, because the virgin grades' marginal cost (non-integrated Nordic wood) will be rising at the same time. That is why I hold the group index flat to slightly down in H2 2027 rather than falling.
  • LPB (about 21% to 23%): the swing grade, now annual. Billerud achieved +8% to 9% at the start of 2024 and SEK 500m to 600m of EBITDA from the early-2025 European round. Jefferies projected LPB price -3% to -5% for 2026. Converters have strong motive: LPB is about 50% of Elopak's costs. The offset is the Nippon Paper Dynawave outage (chemical tank collapse 26 May 2026; about 8% of global LPB supply; JPY 21bn loss guided), which limits buyer switching through the next round. My judgement: 2027 LPB price about -1% to -3%, worth SEK -0.1bn to -0.25bn. A 5% move either way is SEK 0.4bn. This is the single price outcome the Consumer should seek first in the Q4 2026 report on 2 February 2027.
  • Cartonboard and folding boxboard (about 7%): give-back. Jefferies expected FBB -4% for 2026; European FBB operating rates 78% to 81% over 2026 to 2027; Stora Enso's Oulu (750kt) reaches full capacity in 2027; Chinese virgin cartonboard exports doubled to 3.8Mt in 2025. Expect flat to -3%.
  • North American graphic and label paper (about 22%): tariff-supported, durable but small. Price rises from October 2025 after the August 2025 tariffs; tariff pass-through to US import prices about 90% to 95% (ECB). Risks: the October 2026 removal of 22 Canadian tariff lines (Deutsche Bank, 26 October 2026), though Témiscaming's idling keeps Canadian supply below pre-tariff levels; a US demand slowdown. I assume +1% to +2% a year.
  • Market pulp (about 10%): a price-taker. Assume flat.

Contribution to revenue and margin.

2027 2028 2029 to 2030 a year
Price contribution to revenue about +2.0% (after give-back) about +1.0% about +1.0%
Price contribution to EBITDA (gross) about +SEK 0.7bn about +SEK 0.4bn about +SEK 0.45bn
Of which offsetting non-wood inflation about SEK 0.4bn about SEK 0.4bn about SEK 0.4bn
Of which offsetting wood rises 0 about SEK 0.0bn to 0.15bn small
Net margin contribution from price about +0.7 to +0.8 points about 0 to +0.1 points about 0

Why I do not credit price beyond mid-2027. The decisive test is 2021 to 2025: wood doubled and the industry could not pass it through (European EBIT margin 3% in 2025; the group margin target lowered from 17% to 15%). The Wisdom Library test ("does a price rise stick once its cost reverses?") has not been passed; 2026's rises are cost-driven. In the scarce grades, price follows the marginal non-integrated producer's cost, which for wood means Billerud itself, so price will tend to follow wood up in 2028. That makes price a pass-through, not an expansion.

Insight for the Consumer. Price is the largest single line in the 2027 bridge (about SEK 0.7bn gross) and the one with the widest error. The key monitoring points are dated: the LPB round outcome (Q4 2026 report, 2 February 2027), the kraftliner and testliner indices after King's Lynn starts (March to June 2027), and the Q2 2027 price-mix bridge. If the H1 2027 sequential price rise is under 1% and LPB is down more than 3%, FY2027 EBITDA falls towards SEK 4.1bn.

5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7535P-0155
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

F3512th percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: operating leverage is very high. It comes less from fixed costs than from the size of the price, wood and currency variables relative to a thin EBITDA base. Small percentage moves in any of them create a very wide range of EPS outcomes.

Fixed and variable split: Europe is about 75% variable and 25% fixed; North America about 56% variable and 44% fixed. Sensitivities, against FY2026 consensus EBITDA of about SEK 3.46bn:

  • 1% on price: about SEK 0.4bn of EBITDA, about 12% (almost all of a price move drops through).
  • 10% on Nordic wood: about SEK 0.9bn, about 26%.
  • 10% stronger krona: about SEK 0.64bn before hedges, about 18%.
  • 2 points of volume: about SEK 294m (a contribution margin of about 37%), about 8.5%.
  • 1 point of OEE: about SEK 100m.
  • Maintenance timing: about SEK 0.1bn to 0.3bn between years.

From EBITDA to EPS: each SEK 100m of EBITDA is about SEK 0.31 of EPS, which is about 5.5% of FY2027 consensus EPS. Fixed depreciation of about SEK 2.87bn and interest of about SEK 0.3bn act as a second layer of leverage. A 10% change in FY2027 EBITDA (about SEK 0.49bn) moves EPS by about SEK 1.5, or 27%.

My FY2027 range of EBITDA SEK 3.9bn to 5.3bn maps to EPS of about SEK 2.3 to 6.7. The cone is roughly ±50% of central EPS. This leverage is valuable in a recovery and dangerous in a reversal. It is the main reason Billerud's record against estimates is volatile (misses of 24% to 28% at EBITDA level). For the model: build each driver as an explicit line, not as a margin assumption.

P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

C5559th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Billerud's operating leverage is real, but in the forecast period it works through cost lines and price, not through revenue. In 2027 EBITDA rises by about SEK 1.1bn on revenue up only about SEK 1.1bn, an incremental margin of about 100%. That is because the drivers (savings, maintenance, wood, carbon, price) change profit without changing volume. Fixed-cost leverage in the textbook sense (more tonnes over the same fixed base) adds only about SEK 0.1bn to 0.3bn a year, because volume grows only 1% to 2% a year. The second layer of leverage, depreciation of about SEK 2.9bn (about 7% of sales), is what makes EBIT and EPS swing: EBIT rises from about SEK 0.5bn (FY2026) to about SEK 1.6bn (FY2027), a tripling, while EBITDA rises 33%. Against the last six years, the leverage does not take margins back to the historical average until FY2029 to FY2030.

How the leverage is built (the critical numbers).

  • Volume. Europe is about 75% variable and 25% fixed; North America about 56% variable and 44% fixed (Capital Markets Day, 3 December 2024). One point of group volume is worth about SEK 150m to 165m of EBITDA (company: 10% of volume moves pre-tax profit by SEK 1,630m; DNB Carnegie: SEK 1,470m). The AlphaSense report rightly notes that Europe's 25% fixed share understates leverage in deep curtailments, because energy and logistics do not scale down linearly (2023: margin from 19% to 8%).
  • Price. About SEK 0.4bn of EBITDA per 1% of group price. Almost all of a price move drops through.
  • Wood. About SEK 0.9bn per 10% of Nordic pulpwood price (Jefferies); DNB Carnegie puts a 10% fibre move at SEK 1,380m of pre-tax profit (wider fibre definition).
  • Krona. About SEK 640m to 655m per 10% before hedges.
  • Depreciation. About SEK 2.87bn to 2.88bn a year (FactSet: SEK 707m, 719m and 721m in Q1 to Q3 2026). It is fixed in the forecast window because capex (SEK 2.4bn to 2.6bn) runs below it and Gävle only enters service around 2030.

What it does to the line items.

FY2020 FY2021 FY2022 FY2023 FY2024 FY2025 FY2026E FY2027E FY2028E FY2029E FY2030E
Revenue growth -3.4% +8.8% +64.6% -2.6% +4.5% -6.7% -1.9% +2.8% +2.5% +2.5% +2.5%
EBITDA margin (FactSet operating to FY2025; adjusted forecasts) 9.7% 13.9% 18.5% 8.7% 11.6% 9.7% 8.5% 11.0% 11.2% 11.6% 12.0%
EBIT margin 1.6% 6.6% 12.7% 2.0% 5.0% 2.6% 1.3% 4.0% 4.4% 4.9% 5.3%
  • Revenue does not explain profit, historically or in the forecast. In FY2023 revenue fell 2.6% and EBITDA fell 54%; in FY2024 revenue rose 4.5% and EBITDA rose 39%; in FY2025 revenue fell 6.7% and EBITDA fell 22%. The ratio of EBITDA change to revenue change ran between 3x and 20x, because the revenue moves were price moves (and price drops through almost fully) and because wood moved independently. For the Consumer this means revenue growth is a poor monitoring variable. The variables to monitor are the price-cost spread, maintenance and the krona.
  • Margins recover but stay below the six-year average until FY2029 to FY2030. The FY2019 to FY2025 FactSet operating EBITDA margin averaged about 11.9%, and the FY2020 to FY2025 EBIT margin about 5.1%. My forecasts reach those levels only in FY2029 to FY2030, three to four years after the trough. FY2027 at 11.0% sits below FY2024's 11.6% (FactSet operating; 12.3% on the brokers' basis), because FY2024 had surplus carbon allowance income (about SEK 0.47bn) and wood was not yet at its peak.
  • EBIT is the line where leverage bites. With depreciation fixed at about SEK 2.9bn, an EBITDA of SEK 4.5bn gives EBIT of SEK 1.6bn; an EBITDA of SEK 3.9bn gives EBIT of SEK 1.0bn. A 13% swing in EBITDA is a 37% swing in EBIT and a larger one in EPS. This is the mechanical reason the FY2027 EPS cone is roughly ±50%.

Where the leverage could work harder than in my base case. A European capacity closure (by Billerud or a peer) would lift operating rates in cartonboard and kraft, and the remaining mills would absorb fixed costs better. The North American ramp is the cleanest fixed-cost leverage story: the Michigan mills ran at about 75% utilisation in 2025 and above 90% by Q2 2026, with 44% fixed costs. Each extra 50kt of packaging volume, if it replaces idle capacity rather than graphic tonnes, adds perhaps SEK 50m to 100m (my estimate from the 44% fixed share; not disclosed by the company). Neither is large enough to change the shape of 2027.

Where it could work against. The 2023 precedent: European destocking forced curtailments and margins halved. A repeat of a 2% to 3% European volume fall in H2 2027 (seasonal plus recycled competition) costs about SEK 0.3bn to 0.5bn, and the curtailment effect makes it worse than the linear sensitivity implies.

Insight for the Consumer. Operating leverage at Billerud is best modelled as a stack of explicit cost and price lines on top of a nearly fixed depreciation charge, not as a margin on revenue. In my base case about 90% of the 2027 EBITDA increase is explained by named non-volume items (savings, maintenance, wood, carbon, price net of inflation, currency). That makes 2027 more dependable than a volume-led recovery would be in its controllable parts, and less dependable in its price and wood parts.

5c

Financial leverage

2 answers
C-7662P-0258
C-76How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?D6248th percentilefull answer under 4c Financial profile
P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

C+5872nd percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: financial leverage is a minor influence on EPS and a source of resilience, not risk, over the whole forecast window. Net financial items are about SEK 0.15bn to 0.25bn a year, 4% to 5% of EBITDA, so EPS is geared operationally (by depreciation), not financially. Net debt to EBITDA falls from about 1.75x at end-2026 to about 1.2x at end-2027 and stays near 1.0x to 1.1x through 2030 even after the Gävle boiler, because free cash flow roughly matches dividends. The balance sheet therefore absorbs a bear case without stress, but its unused capacity (about SEK 5bn to 7bn of headroom to the 2.5x ceiling from 2028) is not going to be used for buybacks in my base case, so it adds nothing to EPS.

The numbers.

  • Starting point. Net debt SEK 6,020m at Q3 2026 on FactSet's basis (debt SEK 7,412m less cash SEK 1,392m, including leases and other debt); 2.31x trailing on FactSet's EBITDA, or 2.0x on the company's own definition (excluding leases, on adjusted EBITDA). Pensions are small (about SEK 0.5bn of provisions).
  • Path (my forecasts, FactSet basis). End-2026 about SEK 5.9bn (1.75x); end-2027 about SEK 5.3bn (1.19x); end-2028 about SEK 5.0bn (1.07x); end-2029 about SEK 5.3bn (1.06x, Gävle peak spend); end-2030 about SEK 4.9bn (0.93x). This uses my free cash flow (P-08) less dividends paid, and ignores currency translation of US-dollar debt.
  • Interest. I use SEK 150m (FY2026), 220m (FY2027), 200m (FY2028), 230m (FY2029) and 250m (FY2030). The rise in 2029 to 2030 reflects borrowing for Gävle and possibly higher rates on refinancing. Brokers range from SEK 200m (Jefferies) to 220m (DNB Carnegie) for 2027; the compiled consensus cited by DNB Carnegie has SEK 312m, and FactSet consensus EBIT less pre-tax profit implies about SEK 300m.

How it shapes EPS.

  • Each SEK 1bn of net debt costs about SEK 35m to 45m of interest, or about SEK 0.11 to 0.14 of EPS. The whole debt load costs about SEK 0.7 of EPS a year. That is small against the SEK 2.9bn depreciation charge (about SEK 11.5 a share before tax).
  • The broker spread on interest is itself an EPS driver. The range from SEK 200m to 312m in 2027 is worth about SEK 0.35 of EPS. Together with the depreciation spread (SEK 2,788m at DNB Carnegie to SEK 2,980m at Jefferies, worth about SEK 0.6 of EPS), below-EBITDA assumptions explain about one third of the SEK 2.4 gap between the lowest (Jefferies SEK 4.24) and highest (Nordea SEK 6.61) named broker EPS for 2027. The rest is EBITDA (SEK 4,519m to 5,181m, worth about SEK 2.0).
  • No dilution. The share count stays at 248.7m. There is no convertible, and the long-term incentive programmes are small.

How it shapes the balance sheet.

  • Equity. Book value is about SEK 27.6bn (FactSet consensus FY2026, about SEK 111 a share). It grows by retained earnings of only about SEK 0.5bn to 0.8bn a year at a 50% payout, and moves with the krona: translation removed about SEK 1.5bn through other comprehensive income in 2025. A stronger krona would cut book value as well as earnings, which matters because the share price support case rests partly on price to book.
  • Return on equity. About 4% in FY2027 and about 4.5% in FY2028, below any reasonable cost of equity. Financial leverage cannot lift it, because the business earns below its cost of capital at the operating level.
  • Gävle. Above SEK 3bn, decision in 2027, in production around 2030 (Capital Markets Day 2024). I phase about SEK 0.8bn in 2028, 1.4bn in 2029 and 0.9bn in 2030. It holds net debt flat at about SEK 5bn rather than letting it fall to about SEK 3bn. Even in a bear case (FY2027 EBITDA about SEK 3.5bn), leverage would peak at about 1.6x to 1.8x, below the 2.5x ceiling. An equity issue is very unlikely (below 5% in my judgement).

What would change this. A buyback (not in my base case) becomes plausible from 2028 if Gävle is phased or partnered and EBITDA reaches about SEK 5bn; each SEK 1bn of buyback at SEK 89 would retire about 4.5% of the shares and, after the lost interest, add about 2% to 2.5% to EPS. An acquisition in European consolidation would use the headroom; the first set judged M&A unlikely and I agree.

Insight for the Consumer. The balance sheet is the most predictable part of the case and caps the downside in a bad year; it is not a lever on the upside. For the fourth set, the question "will management use the balance sheet?" is the one undated positive catalyst that sits within its control, and the Gävle decision in 2027 is the event that answers it.

5d

Cost-out scope NTM

2 answers
C-7738P-0355
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

F3822nd percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the easily cuttable fixed costs have mostly been cut (SEK 1.5bn plus SEK 0.8bn of programmes, a 15% smaller workforce). What remains reducible is mostly variable (procurement) or structural (capacity). Further meaningful cost reduction now requires closing capacity, not trimming overhead.

By part of the cost base (shares of operating costs in 2025):

  • Wood and fibre (35%). Not cuttable by management. Partly reducible through sourcing mix: more direct purchases from private owners (over 15%), long-term agreements, and lower transport costs through Trätåg rail. It follows the market.
  • Chemicals (13%). Reducible at the margin through renegotiation (SEK 130m of relief in Q1 2026), but inflating with oil and the Middle East.
  • Delivery and logistics (10%). Partly reducible through routing and surcharges; currently inflating.
  • Energy (6%). Mostly self-generated biomass and hedged power, so already low and stable. Gävle and heat-recovery projects reduce it further over time.
  • Personnel and other fixed costs (roughly 25% to 35%, including maintenance). Mostly done: all 650 position reductions were completed by Q1 2026, with SEK 250m more to come in 2027. The rest comes through OEE (+2 points a year, about SEK 200m a year if delivered) and ERP-driven administrative savings.
  • Maintenance (about SEK 1.1bn in 2026). Reducible by about SEK 0.1bn to 0.2bn in a normal year, through scheduling and avoiding overruns.

The remaining structural lever is closing high-cost European capacity (cartonboard and older machines) or, over time, North American graphic machines. That would remove fixed costs and raise utilisation, but no plan has been announced.

P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

C5565th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: about SEK 0.35bn to 0.4bn of self-help reaches EBITDA in 2027 net of wage inflation, roughly nil in 2028 (when Quinnesec's biennial shutdown returns), and only about SEK 0.05bn to 0.1bn a year in 2029 to 2030. The gross figures look larger (SEK 250m of further savings, SEK 0.1bn of maintenance normalisation, SEK 0.1bn to 0.2bn of OEE, SEK 0.1bn of North American non-recurrence), but the record shows that cost programmes at Billerud act as a shield against inflation rather than as a source of margin expansion. The only step change available is a European capacity closure, which I do not put in the base case.

What has been delivered, and what it did to EBITDA. The SEK 1.5bn programme (closed end-2024, one year early; SEK 840m of the benefit came in 2024 against a target of SEK 700m) and the SEK 800m programme (SEK 100m in Q1 2026, SEK 150m each in Q2 and Q3; SEK 550m for 2026; full SEK 800m run-rate from 2027) total about SEK 2.3bn. Over the same period adjusted EBITDA went from SEK 3.3bn (FY2023) to about SEK 3.4bn (FY2026E). The savings were absorbed by wood (which doubled between 2021 and 2025), lost carbon allowance income (about SEK 0.5bn) and price. That is the precedent: savings are real and permanent, but the spread decides the outcome.

What reaches the line items, by year (EBITDA, SEKm).

Item 2027 vs 2026 2028 vs 2027 2029 to 2030 a year Confidence
SEK 800m programme, incremental +250 (Q1 +100, then +50 a quarter, because Q1 2026 had SEK 100m and Q2 to Q3 SEK 150m against SEK 200m a quarter from 2027) 0 0 High
Wage and salary inflation on the remaining base -120 to -180 -120 to -180 -130 to -180 Medium
Maintenance normalisation (2026: about SEK 1.1bn including Quinnesec SEK 171m; 2027: about SEK 1.0bn; 2028: Quinnesec's biennial shutdown returns) +100 -100 0 Medium-high
North American non-recurrence (Quinnesec overrun SEK 50m, Escanaba inefficiencies in Q3 2026) +50 to +100 0 0 Medium
OEE through the Billerud Production System (company: 1 point = SEK 100m; target +2 points a year; no achieved figure disclosed) +100 +100 +100 Low-medium
North American packaging ramp (mix and fixed-cost absorption) +30 +100 +100 to +150 Medium-low
Net self-help reaching EBITDA about +350 to +400 about -50 to +50 about +50 to +100

Why I haircut OEE. The company presents 1 point of OEE as SEK 100m and targets 2 points a year, but discloses no achieved OEE. Metsä Board reports paperboard OEE of 71% against an 85% target, which shows how hard sustained gains are. I credit 1 point a year.

Why savings retention is partial. Jefferies estimates labour is SEK 450m to 600m of the SEK 800m and warns that transport, diesel and chemical costs "will largely offset" the benefit; Q4 2026 alone has a SEK 60m input-cost headwind (two-thirds logistics). I treat non-wood input inflation (about SEK 0.4bn a year) as a separate line, offset by cost-driven price rises (P-04), and treat the savings as reaching EBITDA gross, less wage inflation.

The structural option: a European closure. Billerud's 2026 European volumes of about 2.5Mt are about 0.4Mt below 2021 (Jefferies), roughly a mid-sized machine running idle. Jefferies (September 2023) named Karlsborg (335kt) and Pietarsaari (200kt, non-integrated) as candidates. Precedents: Metsä Board's Tako (210kt) adds EUR 30m of EBITDA for EUR 1m of cash cost; Reno de Medici's Barcelona closure cost about EUR 22.7m to 30m in cash and cut fixed costs with Blendecques by EUR 13.7m. My estimate for Billerud: closing a 200kt to 300kt high-cost unit could add SEK 0.2bn to 0.4bn of EBITDA within 12 to 18 months, for SEK 0.2bn to 0.6bn of cash restructuring, with revenue falling 2% to 4%. I put the chance of an announcement within 18 months at about 25% to 30%. The departure of the EVP Legal & Strategic Projects at end-2026 slightly lowers the chance of a 2027 decision. Not in the base case.

Capex efficiency is the other self-help that reaches the numbers. Capex guidance was cut twice (2025 SEK 3.5bn to 3.1bn; 2026 SEK 2.6bn to 2.4bn), and the base after 2027 is about SEK 2.2bn. This reaches free cash flow, not EBITDA, and Gävle offsets it from 2028.

Insight for the Consumer. The controllable self-help in 2027 is the most dependable block in the forecast (high confidence on about SEK 0.25bn, medium on another SEK 0.1bn to 0.2bn), and it is front-loaded into Q1 2027 against Q1 2026. After 2027 there is no further named programme, so any margin expansion from 2028 must come from the market, the North American ramp or a closure. A new cost programme announcement in 2027 would be a signal that the spread is deteriorating, because that is when Billerud has launched them (2023 and September 2025).

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4733P-0530
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?F3313th percentilefull answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

F308th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: balancing loops dominate the forecast window, and the critical insight is their timing. Three balancing loops (wood give-back through annual LPB and cartonboard pricing, the end of storm salvage wood, and the recycled-supply response to the virgin premium), plus the krona hedge roll-off, all reach the P&L in the same window, H2 2027. Their combined effect is the flat-to-down year-on-year H2 2027 in my quarterly path, and it caps FY2028 EBITDA growth at about 4%. The reinforcing loops (cost programme and OEE; tariffs driving domestic qualification in North America; distress-driven closures) are real but small (about SEK 0.1bn to 0.25bn a year each) and slow, except closures, which are the only loop large enough to change the mid-term picture and are undated.

Each loop, mapped to the line items and years.

Loop Type Operating now? (dated evidence) Line item and year Size in my base case
1. Lower wood → lower board prices in oversupplied grades Balancing Yes. Jefferies (9 January 2026): FBB -4%, LPB -3% to -5%. A former Stora Enso VP (24 December 2025): margins so low customers "do not dare ask". Annual LPB pricing speeds transmission. Revenue and EBITDA, January 2027 rounds -SEK 0.13bn to -0.25bn in 2027
2. Virgin premium → recycled capacity → narrower premium Balancing Starting. Premium EUR 230 a tonne against a ten-year average of EUR 80; King's Lynn (March 2027) and Shotton (2027), 1.45Mt combined, about 3.3% of the 44Mt European market. Delays at Eren; UK export logistics limit reach on the Continent. Price in containerboard and fluting, H2 2027 to 2028 Flattens price from H2 2027; about -SEK 0.1bn to -0.2bn against a no-entry path in 2028
3. Storm salvage → lower wood now → less felling and higher wood later Balancing Yes. Storm Johannes felled about 10 million cubic metres, about 10% of Sweden's annual harvest; Södra and Sveaskog cut price lists in April to May 2026; management guides 2027 average stable "despite potential pressure after storm felling ceases". Precedent: after Gudrun (2005) pulpwood fell 25%; after the 2013 to 2014 storms only 8%. Wood cost, H2 2027 to 2028 +SEK 0.2bn in 2027 (front-loaded), about -SEK 0.25bn in 2028
4. Low returns → closures → higher operating rates Reinforcing (industry) Not yet at scale. Industry needs about 12% of boxboard capacity rationalised; Stora Enso "no plans to close"; prisoner's dilemma. Triggers: distress (FOLBB insolvency, Reno de Medici), Mayr-Melnhof's EUR 2.4bn packaging sale (9 November 2026). Price and volume, 2028 onward 0 in base case; +SEK 0.3bn to 0.6bn if it runs
5. US tariffs → domestic preference → faster qualification and utilisation Reinforcing Yes, but policy-dependent. Utilisation above 90% from 75%; packaging volumes 3kt (Q3 2025) to 11kt (Q3 2026); Témiscaming idled (about 4% of North American SBS); Section 338 lines for Canadian folding carton stock removed (October 2026). North American revenue mix and EBITDA, 2027 to 2030 +SEK 0.03bn (2027), +SEK 0.1bn a year (2028 to 2030)
6. Savings and OEE → lower unit cost → volume without price cuts Reinforcing (weak) Partly. Savings proven; OEE not reported; input inflation "largely offsets" (Jefferies). EBITDA Net about +SEK 0.35bn to 0.4bn in 2027, about nil to +SEK 0.1bn a year after
7. Exporters' recovery → stronger krona → lower SEK revenue Balancing Yes. Riksbank to hedge an extra SEK 100bn of reserves over 12 to 18 months from early 2027 (Morgan Stanley, 20 November 2026); EUR/SEK forecasts 10.40 (Morgan Stanley, Q4 2027) to 11.15 flat (Nordea). Euro hedges 80% in Q1 2027, 38% in Q2 2027. Revenue (-1% a year) and EBITDA -SEK 0.15bn (2027), -SEK 0.05bn to -0.1bn (2028)
8. Carbon: fossil-free mills lose free allowances; possible ETS1 re-entry Balancing (penalises the leader) Yes. Loss of about SEK 507m of net allowance sales (2025); Swedish carbon tax SEK 57m in Q3 2026; ETS1 re-entry proposed from 1 January 2027 (retroactivity not enacted); ETS2 purchases from 2028. Other operating income and costs +SEK 0.1bn (2027), -SEK 0.05bn (2028 onward)

How the loops net out on the line items.

  • 2027: the reinforcing loops (6 and 5) and the lagged tail of loop 3 add about SEK 0.5bn to 0.6bn; the balancing loops (1, 7 and the H2 part of 3) take away about SEK 0.3bn to 0.4bn. The net loop contribution is positive in H1 and negative in H2.
  • 2028: loops 2 and 3 are fully in the P&L, loop 6 has no new programme behind it, and loop 5 is small. The net loop contribution is about -SEK 0.2bn to -0.3bn, which volume growth, price pass-through and the North American ramp just overcome to give about +4% EBITDA.
  • 2029 to 2030: loops settle; margin progress (to 12%) comes from the North American ramp, OEE and volume, plus whatever loop 4 delivers.

Interplay that matters. Loops 1 and 3 interact in Billerud's favour in one respect: if wood rises in H2 2027 (loop 3), the pressure to give back wood relief (loop 1) disappears, because the relief has gone. So the two do not stack fully; together they are a wood-price round trip that leaves the 2028 spread close to the 2026 average. Loops 2 and 7 stack: both hit H2 2027 European margins with no offset.

A ninth loop the Consumer should watch: the estimate-revision loop. FY2027 consensus EPS is up 20% over three months, and the dashboard pins sell-side momentum at A, but EBITDA revisions are only +6% and sales +1%. Rising estimates have supported the share price (+42.9% over six months), which supports the narrative and further upgrades. This loop reverses quickly if Q4 2026 or Q1 2027 shows weaker price; my FY2027 EBITDA is about 9% below FactSet's median.

Insight for the Consumer. The net loop effect is that 2027 earnings look like a recovery in the first half and a plateau in the second, and that 2028 is a year of holding gains rather than adding them. Only the closure loop (4) could turn 2028 into a second leg of improvement, and it has no date.

Billerud's guidance is specific and dependable for the next quarter and the controllable blocks (savings, maintenance, capex, hedges, sensitivities), absent for annual earnings, and aggressive in its undated mid-term target (SEK 6bn of EBITDA, about 25% above the five-year average, under worse input conditions). C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

F4218th percentile

Judgement: Billerud gives no annual earnings guidance, only quarterly building blocks and capex. For FY2026 those blocks are consistent with consensus, possibly slightly below it. For FY2027 the guided blocks explain only part of the consensus EBITDA increase, so the company's implied guidance sits below consensus. The difference must come from price and volume, which management does not guide.

FY2026. The Q4 blocks: maintenance down from SEK 403m to about SEK 80m (a gain of about SEK 320m); input costs of about -SEK 60m; price of about +1% in both regions (about +SEK 100m a quarter); wood flat; and the usual seasonal softness in Q4 (energy, cold weather, year-end destocking, lower European volumes). Starting from Q3's SEK 1,032m, these give a Q4 of about SEK 1.15bn to 1.25bn. Jefferies models SEK 1,177m (FY2026 of SEK 3.40bn). FY2027. The guided blocks are:

  • SEK 250m more savings;

Together these are worth roughly SEK 0.4bn to 0.8bn. Consensus has +SEK 1.47bn (FactSet median) or +SEK 1.2bn (the named-broker median). Jefferies calls consensus "overly ambitious", and DNB Carnegie's FY2027 EPS is below consensus. The gap is why the two Hold-rated brokers sit below the street.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

F3320th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: near-term guidance is reasonable, but the mid-term ambition is not conservative. Adjusted for worse input forces than in the past, it is aggressive.

The SEK 6bn "over a cycle" EBITDA target compares with FactSet operating EBITDA of SEK 3.6bn, 7.9bn, 3.6bn, 5.0bn and 3.9bn in FY2021 to FY2025, an average of about SEK 4.8bn, and with SEK 5.3bn on the brokers' basis in FY2024. The target is about 25% above the five-year average. Yet the input forces are structurally worse than across most of that period:

  • Nordic wood is still roughly 50% above 2021 levels even after a 20% to 30% fall;
  • the carbon position is worse (surplus allowance income gone, ETS2 coming);
  • the krona is stronger than in 2022 to 2024;
  • European oversupply is higher (Oulu, Chinese exports).

Against this, self-help has added about SEK 2.3bn of savings since 2023, and North American fibre is a durable advantage. Netting these, a through-cycle EBITDA of about SEK 5.0bn to 5.5bn looks achievable. SEK 6bn needs a favourable year of wood and price. The European part (SEK 4.3bn to 4.8bn) is the least conservative, given that Europe made about 9% EBITDA margins in 2025. The North American part (SEK 2bn to 2.5bn) is achievable. Near-term blocks (Q4 2026, savings, capex) are realistic.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

A7296th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: five points matter for the Consumer.

  1. Currency and hedge disclosure is unusually detailed (hedge shares and average rates by currency for 15 months). The Consumer can model the hedge roll-off quarter by quarter. EUR flows are 80% hedged in Q1 2027 and 38% in Q2 2027; USD flows are 35% hedged by Q2 2027. Average hedge rates are EUR 10.97 and USD 9.33.
  2. Carbon accounting may be restated retroactively if the Swedish ETS1 amendment applies from 1 January 2026. This could move FY2026 reported results after the event, so watch the Q4 report.
  3. Targets are "over a business cycle" with no date. One Jefferies note frames them as 2027 to 2030. This makes them hard to hold management to.
  4. The dividend policy (at least 50% of net profit) has been applied flexibly. The 2025 payout was about 70% of reported net profit, but the absolute amount was cut, which surprised consensus. Treat dividends as a residual of capex and leverage.
  5. The investor relations calendar. Q4 2026 results on 2 February 2027, the AGM in May, the Gävle decision in 2027, the last Capital Markets Day in December 2024. Sell-side coverage is thin (five named brokers, with no post-Q3 Handelsbanken note). FactSet's consensus median sits above the visible brokers, which means "consensus" figures quoted in the press may overstate the street.
C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

B+6285th percentile

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: estimates were cut deeply and fully across all analysts during 2025 to H1 2026. They are now in an upgrade phase. Company targets were rebased lower once (December 2024) but were not kitchen-sinked, and the SEK 6bn ambition was kept. The setup is "post-reset and recovering", not "sandbagged".

The cuts:

  • FY2026 consensus EPS fell about 80% over twelve months, from about SEK 9.50 to a low of SEK 0.41, and is now SEK 1.26 (FactSet mean).
  • FY2027 EPS is -28% over twelve months and FY2028 -22%. FY2027 EBITDA is -14% over twelve months.
  • Every named broker cut: DNB Carnegie cut 2026 EPS by 38% (February 2026) and by 75% (April 2026); Jefferies cut 2026 EBITDA by 30% (April 2026), and by 18% earlier on FX.

The upgrades since:

  • FY2027 EPS +10% over one month, +20% over three months and +25% over six months.
  • FY2027 EBITDA +3%, +6% and +9%.
  • Target prices +4%, +13% and +18%.
  • DNB Carnegie raised 2026 EPS by 191% (July 2026) and then by 49% (October 2026).
  • Billerud was the top contributor to positive revisions in Sweden in October 2026.
  • The dashboard pins sell-side momentum at A.

Note the composite-signal trap (Wisdom Library): EPS revisions (+20%) far exceed EBITDA revisions (+6%) and sales revisions (+1%). The upgrades are leverage on small EBITDA changes, not broad-based improvement.

Rebasing: the margin target was lowered from 17% to 15%; restructuring charges (SEK 350m) and the Viken write-off were taken; the dividend was cut. But there was no write-down of North America or Europe, and no rebasing of the SEK 6bn target. Estimates are no longer depressed, and the easy upgrades are probably done.

Case simplicity

Group judgement

Risks

D4227th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts are predictable in direction, and for about two quarters ahead in level. Beyond that they are not predictable to better than about plus or minus 15% on EBITDA and plus or minus 50% on EPS, and the errors lean downward. The seven answers fit together as follows.

  • The earlier work holds up (D-01, D-02). After six corrections the central case is unchanged at about SEK 4.5bn of EBITDA for FY2027. About SEK 3.8bn to 3.9bn of it rests on what is already done, and the rest on strengths that are partly shown. Nothing needs a strength the company lacks.
  • The risk is concentrated in three outside variables (D-03), which do the most damage when wood and price separate for supply reasons. That is the shape of the main adverse case for the second half of 2027.
  • The cone is wide and uneven in time (D-04). The first half of 2027 is largely set. The second half and early 2028 are open. I have corrected the second set on one point: two dated forces meet in the second half of 2027, not three. The krona works through a rolling hedge programme, so its effect is spread into 2028.
  • The case can be followed unusually well (D-05, D-06). The company guides in the forecast's own building blocks, the main drivers have public prices, and EBITDA converts to EPS mechanically. A single date, 2 February 2027, settles the LPB round, the dividend, the maintenance schedule and the wood wording.
  • The share price and multiple are mostly the company's own (D-07), so both delivery and disappointment will be felt in full.

My steer to the Consumer.

  1. Present FY2027 as SEK 4.5bn of EBITDA and SEK 4.4 of EPS, with a four-in-five range of SEK 3.8bn to 5.2bn and SEK 2.2 to 6.6. Do not present SEK 3.9bn as a floor.
  2. Frame the forecast as the second-half 2026 run-rate (SEK 4.4bn a year) held and slightly improved. That is the most compact and most testable statement of it, and it shows exactly what consensus needs in addition: about SEK 0.5bn.
  3. Separate two kinds of predictability. Good tracking (high) is not the same as a narrow range of outcomes (low). This case has the first without the second.
  4. Expect the next two reports to look strong against a year earlier whatever the truth about the second half. They test the first half of our forecast, which is not in dispute. The dispute is tested from July 2027.
  5. Nothing on the horizon would make the case more predictable. Longer LPB contracts, wood ownership or a capacity closure would, and none is planned.
C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

D4546th percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the perimeter is narrow in production geography and moderately broad in products. That combination makes earnings predictable in structure but unpredictable in level, because a few shared variables (Nordic wood, the krona, European board prices) drive most of the group.

Production: nine units in three countries (Sweden, with five mills, plus Finland's Pietarsaari and two Michigan mills), split into two regions. Products: about eight lines:

  • LPB (about 21% to 23% of sales);
  • graphic paper (about 22%);
  • kraft and specialty paper (16%);
  • containerboard, fluting and liner (13%);
  • pulp (10%);
  • sack (9%);
  • cartonboard (7%).

Sales go to more than 100 countries, with Europe 45% to 63% and North America 29% to 39% depending on the measure.

Changing: graphic paper is shrinking by about 5% a year; North American packaging is growing; non-core units (Managed Packaging, Wisconsin Rapids, Viken) have been removed; a European closure is possible.

Predictability: the regions hedge each other partly (North America has local costs, a dollar natural hedge and tariff protection), but Europe carries all of the wood and krona risk. The narrowing towards virgin packaging grades increases exposure to the fresh-fibre cycle. The perimeter is fine for modelling; the variables are the problem.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

D7046th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the expected change is plausible, because it asks the business to return to a level it has earned before and is already earning, using strengths it has shown. About SEK 3.8bn to 3.9bn of the SEK 4.5bn forecast for FY2027 rests on actions already taken or prices and costs already set. The remaining SEK 0.6bn to 0.7bn must still be delivered in 2027, and it rests on strengths that are only partly demonstrated: raising prices further while costs are falling, clean North American shutdowns, and efficiency gains that have never been reported. Nothing in the base case needs a strength the business would have to acquire. The mid-term target of SEK 6bn does: it needs either wood the company does not own or industry capacity it does not control.

The level is one the business has delivered. The FY2027 margin of 11.0% compares with 12.3% in FY2024 (brokers' basis) and a FY2019 to FY2025 average of about 11.9%. The second half of 2026 is already running at SEK 4.4bn a year, and Region Europe reached a 12% margin in Q3 2026. No new market, product, acquisition or technology is needed.

Strength by strength, against the FY2027 bridge.

What the forecast needs Worth in FY2027 (SEKm) Demonstrated?
Cost programme reaches SEK 800m +250 Yes. The SEK 1.5bn programme closed a year early; all staff reductions for this one were done by April 2026; SEK 100m, 150m and 150m were delivered in Q1 to Q3. The last step, from SEK 150m to 200m a quarter, is not yet visible, but it is mostly the end of notice periods.
Maintenance as scheduled, no Quinnesec shutdown +100 Yes in Europe. Not in North America: Quinnesec overran by SEK 50m and Escanaba cost SEK 104m against SEK 90m in 2026.
Prices already achieved are held (the Q4 2026 exit level against the 2026 average) about +400 Yes for kraftliner, sack and fluting in 2026. Holding price while wood falls has not been shown: in 2026 LPB prices fell, and in cartonboard relief is given back.
Further price rises in the first half of 2027 about +450 Partly. Management guides positive pricing into the first half; kraftliner (+EUR 80 a tonne) and sack (+EUR 50) rises reach contracts with a lag. But management calls the rises "cost-driven", and the group has never widened its spread through price in a year of falling costs.
Lagged wood relief in the first half about +275, of which about 125 reverses in the second half Not a strength. Billerud is a price-taker with under 5% self-sufficiency. The first-half benefit is already in price lists.
OEE, North American non-recurrence, volume +200 Weakly. OEE is unreported; North American execution has disappointed; European volume is refill, not use.
Absorbing inflation and currency -475 A cost, not a strength. Hedging and treasury are well run.

What past and present delivery say. On what it controls, the company delivers early or on time: two cost programmes, two capex cuts, the cancelled SEK 7bn conversion, cash conversion of 97% and 127% in Q2 and Q3 2026, and the Frövi recovery boiler (SEK 2.6bn) on time and on budget. On outcomes set by markets it has missed, and by more than it has beaten: EBITDA misses of 24% to 28% against beats of 3% to 16%; a 2025 margin of 10% against a target of 17% that was then cut to 15%. Present delivery is the best evidence for the case: two beats in a row, EBITDA doubled between Q1 and Q3 2026, and the 2026 savings target raised from SEK 500m to 550m. Region North America, which carries the conversion to packaging, still missed consensus by 13% in Q3.

Two strengths that are better evidenced than the earlier sets allowed.

  • The North American packaging ramp to about 80kt in 2027 needs only the pace already shown. Quarterly volumes were 3kt, 4kt, 6kt, 9kt and 11kt from Q3 2025 to Q3 2026, an increase of 2kt to 3kt each quarter. The same pace gives about 80kt to 85kt in 2027. The target of 300kt by 2030 needs the quarterly increase to rise to about 4kt, in a market where Sappi's Somerset machine adds at least another 100kt in its 2027 fiscal year. The base case does not depend on the target.
  • Large-project execution in Sweden. Frövi is a direct precedent for the Gävle boiler.

The one strength the case would need and does not have. Retaining a favourable price-cost spread once the cost that justified it reverses. The 2021 to 2025 record (wood doubled; European EBIT margin 3% in 2025) is the decisive evidence. This is why the forecast stops improving after mid-2027, and why I agree with the second set that 2028 holds gains and does not add to them.

A caution on the evidence. The AlphaSense report commissioned for this question is a sector-level study dated August 2026. It repeats material the first set already used and adds nothing on Billerud's demonstrated strengths. This answer therefore rests on the earlier sets and the other reports.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?D4034th percentilefull answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

F2815th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the cone is wide and skewed to the downside, and the case is predictable in direction but not in level. For FY2027 I put central EBITDA at about SEK 4.5bn and the range that should hold four outcomes in five at SEK 3.8bn to 5.2bn (EPS about SEK 2.2 to 6.6 around 4.4). That is wider than the second set's cone, because the company's own record of forecast error demands it. Within the 18 months the width is very unequal: the first half of 2027 is largely set (about plus or minus 10%), while the second half of 2027 and the first quarter of 2028 are open (about plus or minus 25%). The skew around our own numbers is mildly negative. Around consensus it is strongly negative: I give about a one-in-four chance that FY2027 EBITDA reaches FactSet's median.

Why the cone is this wide.

  • Built from the bridge. Combining the ranges in D-03 gives about plus or minus SEK 0.65bn around SEK 4.5bn.
  • Checked against the record. On 1 February 2026 Jefferies, then the most cautious named broker, cut its FY2026 EBITDA forecast by 18% to SEK 4.2bn. The year will end at about SEK 3.4bn, a further 19% lower, with eleven months of the year then still ahead, as eleven months of FY2027 will be on 2 February 2027. FY2026 consensus EPS fell about 80% in twelve months. FY2027 consensus EPS is 31% lower than twelve months ago and 25% higher than six months ago. The named brokers today span SEK 4.5bn to 5.2bn on FY2027 EBITDA and SEK 4.24 to 6.61 on EPS for a year that starts in six weeks.
  • The second set gave 60% to the band from SEK 4.0bn to 4.9bn. I keep roughly that, and state the wider band explicitly so that the memo does not present SEK 3.9bn as a floor. It is the level supported by what is already done (D-02), not a limit on what a bad year can do.

My distribution for FY2027 adjusted EBITDA. Below SEK 3.8bn: about one chance in eight. SEK 3.8bn to 4.2bn: about 20%. SEK 4.2bn to 4.7bn: about 35%. SEK 4.7bn to 4.92bn: about 12%. At or above SEK 4.92bn (FactSet's median): about 20% to 25%, including about one chance in twelve of more than SEK 5.2bn.

The cone by period (adjusted EBITDA, SEKm).

Period Central Plausible range What sets the width
Q4 2026 about 1,160 1,080 to 1,280 Guided blocks; seasonal volume and energy. Brokers imply 1,080 to 1,300.
Q1 2027 1,160 to 1,250 1,050 to 1,330 LPB round; size of the price rise; winter energy. Hedge rates known.
Q2 2027 about 1,090 950 to 1,230 European maintenance season; first effect of UK capacity on containerboard.
H2 2027 about 2,250 1,700 to 2,750 Wood after salvage; the virgin premium; hedge rates set during the first half; cartonboard give-back.
Q1 2028 about 1,130 800 to 1,400 The 2028 LPB round; ETS2; wood.
Six quarters about 6,800 about 5,900 to 7,600

Why the first half of 2027 is narrow. Four things are fixed before or early in the period. Euro and dollar flows are about 70% hedged for Q1 and 50% for Q2 at known rates. Wood cost in the accounts lags price lists by three to six months, and the lists for the first half point to stability. The savings and the maintenance schedule are set. And LPB prices, once the January round is done, are fixed for the calendar year. The last point is an insight the earlier sets did not draw out: annual LPB pricing makes Billerud's earnings less stable from one year to the next than Stora Enso's, but more stable within a year, and the outcome is known in the first report of the year.

Why the second half is wide, with my correction to the second set. The second set placed three dated forces in the second half of 2027. I find two. Wood: the comparable storm precedent implies a rebound of under 10%, which over a half-year is worth up to SEK 0.45bn; my central case is a rise of about 4%. The virgin premium: at EUR 230 a tonne it is nearly three times its ten-year average of EUR 80; Jefferies expects recycled prices to fall EUR 20 to 30 a tonne in the first half of 2027 and the virgin premium to stay high, but the conversion wave of 2019 and 2020 did eventually pull kraftliner down. The third force, the hedge "roll-off", is not a dated event (D-01). The krona instead widens the cone for late 2027 and for 2028 by whatever it does in the first half of 2027. The second-half cone is therefore slightly less crowded than the second set drew it, and the 2028 cone slightly wider.

The skew, and its sources. Downside is the longer tail because: misses have been larger than beats (24% to 28% against 3% to 16%); the adverse case is a decoupling that demand does not hedge (D-03); mill curtailments make volume losses worse than proportional; concentrated buyers cap the upside in LPB and cartonboard; and costs rise by contract (wages, logistics) while prices must be negotiated. The upside sources are real but each needs a favourable outcome: LPB up to 3% higher, wood staying at its low through 2027 (DNB Carnegie's view), gas staying high enough to keep recycled mills curtailed, a competitor's closure, and a firmer dollar (Nordea expects USD/SEK to rise into mid-2027).

What is and is not predictable.

  • That FY2027 EBITDA exceeds FY2026: about 95%.
  • That Q1 2027 EBITDA is more than 50% above Q1 2026: very likely.
  • That second-half 2027 EBITDA exceeds second-half 2026: little better than even.
  • That FY2028 exceeds FY2027: about 60%.
  • The level of FY2027 EPS: only to within about plus or minus 50%.
D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

A7596th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: disclosure is very helpful for monitoring this case over six months, for two reasons. The company reports and guides in the same building blocks the forecast is made of, and the variables that carry most of the risk have public prices that can be followed monthly without the company. There are two limits. The three facts that would settle the 2027 debate (the LPB price outcome, the wood cost actually paid, and the rates at which hedges are being renewed) are disclosed only indirectly. And the six-month window resolves the first-half questions, not the second-half ones: the first evidence on wood after salvage and on the virgin premium arrives in public price data from about May 2027 and in the accounts in October 2027.

What the company gives each quarter. Shipments in kilotonnes; the sales bridge (volume, price, mix, currency); EBITDA by region; maintenance cost by mill, against plan; savings delivered; North American packaging tonnes; the hedge table by currency and quarter for 15 months; leverage and cash conversion; and an outlook for the next quarter in percent or kronor (price by region, input costs, wood direction, maintenance).

The six-month calendar, and what to read on each date.

Date Event What it tells us
15 December 2026 Stora Enso extraordinary general meeting on its forest listing Sector read-across; attention on who owns wood.
December to February Riksbank decisions (Nordea expects 2.25% in February 2027) The krona rates at which second-half 2027 hedges are being struck.
January 2027 Forest-owner and Sveaskog price lists; SCA and Holmen reports Wood for the first half; whether "no further decreases" (SCA) has become increases.
2 February 2027 Q4 2026 report See the list below.
March 2027 Annual report; Palm's King's Lynn start-up Sensitivities, wood agreements, emissions; the first test of the virgin premium.
Late April 2027 Q1 2027 report The first quarter with 2027 LPB prices, the full savings rate and the new carbon regime; the Q2 outlook.
May 2027 Annual general meeting Dividend paid; any comment on Gävle or closures.

What to read on 2 February 2027, with thresholds.

  • Group adjusted EBITDA. About SEK 1.15bn to 1.25bn is consistent with the guided blocks. Below about SEK 1.05bn would mean a block failed.
  • Region Europe margin. Q3's 12% was earned with about SEK 300m of European maintenance; Q4 has SEK 80m. The blocks therefore imply about 13%. I recommend replacing the first set's tripwire of 10% with 12%. A margin below 12% would mean the underlying margin fell in the quarter. A 10% margin would require a shortfall of about SEK 0.2bn, by which point the thesis would already be visibly in trouble.
  • LPB. Any statement on 2027 prices. If there is none, the European price and mix lines in the Q1 report will show the outcome.
  • Wood. Whether "stable on average against 2026" is repeated, and the guided direction for Q1.
  • The hedge table. Read the rates newly added for Q2 to Q4 2027, not the percentages. They show what the krona is doing to second-half revenue.
  • The 2027 maintenance schedule and capex. Whether capex stays at SEK 2.6bn, and whether it includes anything for Gävle.
  • The dividend proposal, against SEK 2.00 in consensus.
  • North American packaging tonnes. The demonstrated pace implies 13kt to 14kt in Q4 (11kt in Q3).
  • Savings. SEK 150m in Q4 completes the SEK 550m for 2026; the statement that SEK 200m a quarter starts in Q1.

Public data that tracks the drivers between reports. Swedish pulpwood price lists and the forest agency's statistics. Kraftliner and testliner indices and the premium between them (monthly). Sack paper prices (quarterly). EUR/SEK and USD/SEK. European gas (EUR 78 to 100 per MWh this autumn) and EU allowance prices. US price announcements for bleached board and linerboard. Peers' reports in the same weeks. Few investment cases can be followed this closely from outside the company.

What is not disclosed. Prices by grade and the LPB contract outcome. Wood cost per cubic metre, and the share bought on fixed or indexed terms. EBITDA by product or mill. OEE achieved. The tonnes of allowances to be bought and the cost of ETS2. The margin on North American packaging tonnes and the number of qualified customers. The cost and phasing of Gävle. And the make-up of the "Other" segment, which is about SEK -0.6bn a year, includes hedging results and wood trading, and is uneven (on Jefferies' Europe figure for Q1 it ran at about -49, -163 and -174 in Q1 to Q3 2026).

The insight for the Consumer. One date, 2 February 2027, removes a large part of the first-half uncertainty at once: the LPB round, the dividend, the maintenance schedule, capex and the wood wording. Monitoring over six months is therefore well served. But a clean February report says little about the second half, which is where our forecast and consensus part company. The earliest warnings for the second half will not come from Billerud. They will come from the spring wood price lists and the kraftliner index after the UK start-ups.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

B+6285th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the trace from what the company says to earnings per share is clear and largely arithmetic for the next two or three quarters, and it breaks down beyond that. Near-term statements are made in kronor or percent on named lines, and the path from EBITDA to EPS is nearly mechanical, because depreciation, interest, tax and the share count hardly move: each SEK 100m of EBITDA is SEK 0.31 of EPS. The mid-term targets do not trace at all. The external transmission, from wood, board prices, currency and carbon to EPS, is clear in direction, size and lag, but opaque at three joints: LPB pricing terms, the mix of wood contracts, and the "Other" segment.

The internal trace.

What the company says Driver Line To EPS Verifiable?
SEK 800m of savings from 2027 (SEK 250m more than 2026) Headcount and fixed costs, mostly Europe Fixed costs, Region Europe EBITDA +SEK 0.78 Yes, reported each quarter
Maintenance by mill and quarter Shutdown schedule Regional EBITDA SEK 0.31 per SEK 100m Yes, with cost against plan
Price about +1% in both regions Selling prices Revenue; almost all reaches EBITDA About SEK 0.31 for each 1% held for one quarter Yes, in the sales bridge, but not by grade
Input costs about SEK 60m higher Logistics, chemicals, energy Variable costs -SEK 0.19 Partly
Wood stable on average in 2027 Pulpwood cost Variable costs; SEK 0.9bn per 10% SEK 2.8 per 10% Only as a direction
Capex SEK 2.6bn; base SEK 2.2bn after 2027 Investment Cash flow; depreciation later None before 2029 Yes
300kt of North American packaging by 2030 Volume and mix Region North America revenue Not given Tonnes yes, margin no
OEE up two points a year; one point is SEK 100m Machine uptime Costs and volume SEK 0.31 per point No: no achieved figure is disclosed
EBITDA above SEK 6bn over a cycle Regional ranges only None None No bridge and no date

A test of the trace against 2026. In Q3 2026 the disclosed blocks (wood relief, a 7% sequential rise in European volume, price rises in fluting and containerboard, steady savings) explain almost all of Region Europe's rise from about SEK 425m to SEK 734m. In Q1 and Q2 2026 they did not. Q1 missed consensus by 24% and Q2 beat it by 16%, both on European price, mix and volume within the quarter, which the company does not guide. The blocks are necessary for a forecast but not sufficient. The untraced part is where the surprises have come from.

The external transmission, with its lags.

  • Wood. Forest-owner price lists (about two-thirds of European purchases) reach the accounts after three to six months, because the wood inventory turns in two to three months. Size: SEK 0.9bn of EBITDA per 10%.
  • Board and paper prices. Containerboard and kraftliner follow published indices within one to three months. Sack paper resets quarterly to half-yearly. LPB resets once a year, in January. US graphic paper moves by announced increases. Size: about SEK 0.4bn per 1% across the group.
  • Currency. Through a rolling 15-month hedge programme, so with a lag of about two to four quarters. Size: SEK 640m per 10% before hedges (euro 300, dollar 260, sterling 80).
  • Carbon. An annual regime: the allowance price times a small, undisclosed volume of fossil emissions, about SEK 100m a year.
  • Gas. Indirect: each EUR 10 per MWh moves the recycled cost curve by EUR 12 to 15 a tonne (Jefferies), which supports testliner prices and the virgin premium.
  • Capacity. New recycled machines and Oulu act on operating rates and then on price, over quarters, not weeks.

The three opaque joints.

  1. LPB terms. The chief executive says all volume is on annual price terms. Jefferies describes SIG's supply agreements as multi-year with annual inflation adjusters and a larger renegotiation every three years. Both may be true of different customers, but an outsider cannot tell how far the 2027 price follows an index and how far it is negotiated. On 22% of sales, with SEK 80m to 90m of EBITDA per 1%, this is the largest single gap in the trace.
  2. Wood contracts. The terms with Sveaskog and under the felling-rights agreement to 2034 are not disclosed, so the share of wood that follows spot lists is an estimate.
  3. "Other" and the lines below EBITDA in 2026. "Other" moves by more than SEK 100m between quarters. Reported net income for the first nine months of 2026 (SEK -71m) cannot be reconciled from adjusted EBITDA without tax and financial items the company does not explain in the blocks.

The insight for the Consumer. Because the path from EBITDA to EPS is mechanical and thin (EBIT is only about 4% of sales), an untraced SEK 100m is 7% of FY2027 EPS. The lag structure sets how far ahead EPS can be traced from public data: wood and currency two to three quarters, LPB one calendar year at a time, containerboard one to three months. That is the structural reason why predictability here is front-loaded, and why it cannot be extended by better analysis, only by time passing.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D2536th percentile

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: sentiment risk is high now, because the stock has re-rated sharply and is crowded into one early-cycle narrative, while the market regime is risk-off beneath the index.

The position of the stock:

  • +43% over six months and the 81st percentile of the universe on relative strength.
  • 24-month forward P/E of 13.6x, the 91st percentile of five years. The dashboard valuation letter is D and the technical letter is D.
  • The 200-day average has risen in only one of the last twelve months.
  • Billerud is the sector note's "purest way to own" the fresh-fibre scarcity story.

Sudden sentiment risks:

  1. The scarcity story cracking at its first dated test. The start of UK recycled capacity (King's Lynn, March 2027) and any fall in kraftliner or testliner price indices would hit the purest exposure hardest.
  2. Read-across from peers. Mondi, Smurfit Westrock or Stora Enso profit warnings, a failed Stora Enso forest listing (EGM 15 December 2026, first trading by end-March 2027), or Holmen and SCA misses. Holmen missed by 14% in Q3 2026, and SCA was downgraded to Hold on 26 October 2026.
  3. A wood-price headline (Swedish price lists rising) before it reaches the P&L.
  4. The macro regime. The FfF reading is Fragility at Double Underweight and Momentum at Underweight. Mid-cap Swedish cyclicals de-rate first in a risk-off move, and higher bond yields (US 10-year near 5%) compress cyclical multiples.
  5. Further rating moves. Two of five named brokers are already at Hold (Jefferies, DNB Carnegie), and the buy share fell 10 points over three months.
  6. The return of short interest. It doubled to 2.17% after the H1 2025 miss.

A combined sector de-rating would take the stock back towards book-value support (SEK 60s) regardless of Q4 delivery.

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C-5053rd percentile

Judgement: there is little record of upgrading financial guidance, because there is almost none to upgrade. The pattern under Ivar Vatne:

  • Operational and cost targets were raised or beaten: the SEK 1.5bn programme closed a year early; 2026 savings rose from SEK 500m to SEK 550m; staff reductions were completed ahead of plan.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F3318th percentile

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: the record is volatile and roughly two-sided, with misses larger than beats. Billerud beats when self-help and cost relief dominate a quarter, and misses when European volume or price disappoints. In comparable early-recovery conditions (H2 2023 to 2024), an initial run of beats was followed by misses once volumes and prices failed to follow.

  • Last 12 months.
  • Q4 2025: missed (sales 8% below consensus; European volumes -12% year on year).
  • Q1 2026: missed by about 24% at EBITDA.
  • Q2 2026: beat by about 16% against consensus (10% against DNB Carnegie).
  • Q3 2026: beat (EBITDA +3%, EBIT +9%, EPS +46%, but North America -13%).
  • Dividend: missed (SEK 2.00 against SEK 3.30 to 3.50).
  • Three years.
  • June 2023: a profit warning (Q2 2023 EBITDA guided to SEK 100m to 200m).
  • Q3 2023: a strong beat.
  • Q4 2023: missed by 6%.
  • Q4 2024: beat.
  • Q2 2025: missed by 28%.
  • Q3 2025: beat.
  • Five years. Beats in the 2021 to 2022 upcycle, then the 2023 collapse.

Against its own guidance, quarterly direction has mostly been right. Against its own targets (the 17% margin, 11% ROCE), it has not delivered and lowered one of them. Size pattern: misses of 24% to 28%, beats of 3% to 16%. For the model, put more weight on downside error, especially in Q4 and Q1, which carry seasonal weakness, energy costs and customers' year-end inventory management.

P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F358th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the forecasts should carry high weight for the controllable lines (savings, maintenance budgets, capex, hedges, share count, dividend policy) and low weight for the lines set by the market (price, wood, the krona), and they should be leaned down relative to FactSet consensus. Billerud's record is two-sided but asymmetric: misses of 24% to 28% at EBITDA level, beats of 3% to 16%. The misses come in Q4 and Q1 and when European price or volume disappoints. My base case already leans down (FY2027 EBITDA SEK 4.5bn against FactSet's SEK 4.92bn median and the named-broker median of SEK 4.65bn), and I judge the remaining skew around my own numbers to be modestly negative for FY2027 and more clearly negative for FY2028.

The record, separated into what management controls and what it does not.

  • Controllable, delivered or beaten: the SEK 1.5bn programme closed a year early, and 2024's benefit was SEK 840m against SEK 700m targeted; SEK 800m programme staff cuts done by April 2026 and the 2026 target raised from SEK 500m to 550m; capex guidance cut twice; quarterly outlooks directionally right (Q3 2026 "positive pricing and stable markets" was delivered); hedging and maintenance disclosed and broadly met.
  • Controllable, missed: North American shutdowns (Quinnesec SEK 171m, SEK 50m over plan, Q2 2026; Escanaba SEK 104m against about SEK 90m planned, Q3 2026, with lingering inefficiencies); the 2018 maintenance overruns; KM7 start-up costs of SEK 450m in 2020.
  • Market-driven, missed: profit warning June 2023; Q4 2023 -6%; Q2 2025 -28%; Q4 2025 (sales 8% below consensus); Q1 2026 -24%; the 17% margin target abandoned; ROCE of 4% against 11%; the dividend for 2025 cut well below consensus (SEK 2.00 against SEK 3.30 to 3.50).
  • Comparable conditions: in the last early-recovery phase (H2 2023 to 2024) an initial run of beats was followed by misses once volume and price failed to follow. Today's position (two beats after a deep miss, estimates rising) matches that pattern.

Weight by horizon.

Horizon Weight I would give my forecast Why
Q4 2026 (reported 2 February 2027) High Built from guided blocks: maintenance SEK 80m, input costs -SEK 60m, price +1%, wood flat. My SEK 1.16bn is close to Jefferies' SEK 1,177m. FactSet's FY2026 median implies about SEK 1.24bn, which needs everything to go right.
H1 2027 Medium-high Savings, maintenance, hedges (80% EUR in Q1) and the lagged wood benefit are largely locked. The unknowns are the LPB round and the strength of the H1 price rise.

My probability distribution for FY2027 adjusted EBITDA (judgement). Below SEK 4.0bn: about 20% (a joint wood, krona and European price setback, which the first set put at about one in six). SEK 4.0bn to 4.5bn: about 30%. SEK 4.5bn to 4.9bn: about 30%. Above SEK 4.9bn, FactSet's median: about 20% (needs wood to stay at the Q4 2026 exit level and LPB to hold flat). The median is about SEK 4.5bn; the downside tail (to SEK 3.5bn) is longer than the upside tail (to SEK 5.3bn).

Direction of lean, line by line. Lean down: EBITDA (price and wood), revenue (krona translation; my FY2027 revenue SEK 40.9bn is below every named broker, whose range is SEK 40.9bn to 42.2bn), dividend (FY2026). Lean neutral: capex, depreciation, share count. Lean up: net financial items (lower than consensus) and leverage (falls faster than many expect because cash conversion has run at about 100% in the first nine months of 2026).

Insight for the Consumer. The track record says Billerud's numbers fail where the market decides them, and the forecast calendar puts the market-decided items into H2 2027 and 2028. So the predictability of my 18-month forecast is front-loaded: the first two to three reported quarters should validate it with some confidence, and the uncertainty widens sharply from the Q2 2027 report (July 2027) onward.

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

D4028th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts describe a cyclical recovery that is real, front-loaded and largely already expected, followed by a plateau. FY2027 EBITDA rises about a third to SEK 4.5bn on savings, maintenance normalisation, lagged wood relief and price in scarce grades; EPS quadruples to about SEK 4.4 because depreciation (about SEK 2.9bn) is fixed. But almost all of the year-on-year improvement lands in H1 2027. In H2 2027 three balancing loops and the hedge roll-off arrive together (salvage wood ends, UK recycled capacity starts, the krona hedges fall away), and FY2028 grows only about 4%. By FY2029 to FY2030 the business returns to a neutral-cycle EBITDA of SEK 5.0bn to 5.3bn, margins of 11.6% to 12.0%, and EPS of SEK 5.9 to 6.5: the post-Verso average after the loss of carbon allowance income, plus banked self-help.

Three judgements matter most for the Consumer:

  • Consensus is high for FY2027 and FY2028. My EBITDA is 9% and 11% below FactSet's medians, and my EPS 22% and 21% below. The gap is explained mainly by the reading of management's flat-wood guidance and by price retention in the annual LPB round, not by any doubt about the controllable items.
  • Predictability is front-loaded. The controllable blocks (about SEK 0.5bn) are high-confidence and make SEK 3.9bn a sensible floor for FY2027. Everything above that floor depends on price, wood and the krona, whose dated tests fall between February and October 2027.
  • The balance sheet is strong and getting stronger, but it is not a lever. Net debt falls to about 1.0x to 1.2x EBITDA, and the Gävle boiler absorbs the free cash that could have funded buybacks. Free cash flow of about SEK 0.4bn to 1.1bn a year covers a 50% payout and little more. Returns stay below the cost of capital throughout the forecast.

The overall effect of the forecasts on the investment case is mildly negative at today's price: the near-term numbers will look good, but the mid-term numbers do not support the SEK 6bn target or the current 24-month forward P/E (13.6x, the 91st percentile of five years), and the risks are skewed down from mid-2027.

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

C+6272nd percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: below operating profit, the forecast years are cleaner and cheaper than the last four years. Interest falls to about SEK 0.2bn from SEK 0.3bn to 0.4bn in 2023 to 2025, the tax rate normalises at about 22% after reported rates of -55% and 58%, items affecting comparability shrink after the 2025 to 2026 restructuring, and there are no buybacks, issues or minorities. These items add about SEK 0.3 to 0.5 of EPS relative to a FY2023 to FY2025-style conversion. Free cash flow becomes consistently positive (about SEK 0.4bn to 1.1bn a year) after three years averaging about SEK -0.6bn, but it is held down by capex (Gävle from 2028), a working-capital rebuild and dividends, so cash after dividends is roughly zero over 2027 to 2030. Billerud does not become a cash-return story in the forecast window.
