Each item, and what it does.

  • Depreciation. Flat at about SEK 2.87bn to 2.88bn to 2028, because capex runs below it. It rises from 2029 as Evolution and then Gävle assets come into service. The broker spread here is wide: SEK 2,788m (DNB Carnegie) to SEK 2,980m (Jefferies, rising to SEK 3,040m in 2028). I am between the two, close to FactSet consensus (SEK 2,882m). Each SEK 100m is SEK 0.31 of EPS.
  • Interest. Lower than the last three years because net debt falls (SEK 6.0bn to about SEK 5.0bn to 5.3bn) and because the 2024 to 2025 figures included pension and other financial items. Billerud does not disclose a sensitivity of finance costs to the Gävle borrowing; this is a gap.
  • Tax. About 22% on adjusted profit (Swedish 20.6%, US federal and state about 25%, mix). Reported rates were distorted by losses and deferred tax in 2023 and 2025. I see no structural leakage. A low-profit 2026 could still show an odd reported rate.
  • Items affecting comparability. The first nine months of 2026 show reported FactSet EBITDA of SEK 1,914m against adjusted SEK 2,218m, a gap of SEK 304m (the Viken write-off of about SEK 47m to 50m in Q1, plus restructuring and other items). Reported 2026 net income will therefore be close to zero even though adjusted EPS is about SEK 1.1. That matters for the dividend (point below) and for headlines. From 2027 I assume small items unless a closure is announced, which would bring SEK 0.2bn to 0.6bn of charges.
  • Carbon. Treated above EBITDA (other operating income). The FY2024 and FY2025 bases included about SEK 0.47bn and 0.51bn of allowance sales that do not return; this is the main reason the FY2024 EPS of SEK 6.24 is not a like-for-like "normal".
  • Minorities and associates. None of significance.
  • Share count, buybacks and issuance. 248.7m shares, unchanged in every year. No buyback in the base case before 2028, and none in my numbers after that because Gävle absorbs the free cash. A buyback is optional upside (P-02).
  • Dividend. FY2026 SEK 1.50 (paid 2027): a payout far above 50% of adjusted EPS, but the board has paid above 100% at earlier troughs (FY2020: SEK 4.01 on reported EPS of SEK 3.04; FY2023: SEK 2.00 on SEK 1.95). I set it below consensus SEK 2.00 and DNB Carnegie's SEK 1.75 because reported profit is near zero and Gävle is pending. The range is SEK 1.00 to 2.00. Then about 50% of adjusted EPS: SEK 2.15 (FY2027), 2.55, 2.90, 3.25.

Free cash flow, and how it compares. My definition: adjusted EBITDA, less cash items affecting comparability, capex (including intangibles), net interest, tax paid, lease payments (about SEK 150m) and the change in working capital.

  • FY2026 about SEK 0.71bn: EBITDA 3,378, restructuring cash about -200, capex -2,400, interest -150, tax about -100, leases -150, working capital release about +330 (13.6% to 13.0% of revenue). FactSet consensus SEK 681m.
  • FY2027 about SEK 0.95bn: working capital rebuilds by about SEK 270m as prices and volumes rise (Jefferies has -SEK 225m). Consensus SEK 1,432m; DNB Carnegie SEK 1,005m.
  • FY2028 about SEK 0.85bn and FY2029 about SEK 0.4bn: Gävle phasing (about SEK 0.8bn and 1.4bn). FY2030 about SEK 1.1bn.
  • Against history: FactSet free cash flow was SEK 2,621m (FY2022), -1,734m, 164m and -198m (FY2023 to FY2025), with dividends partly debt-funded in 2023 and 2025. The forecast is better because capex is lower than in 2022 to 2023 and EBITDA is higher than in 2023 and 2025. But the yield is modest: about 4% on the SEK 22.4bn market value in 2027 to 2028, falling to about 2% in 2029.
  • Working capital quality. Supplier finance payables fell from SEK 711m (2024) to SEK 327m (2025), with payment terms of 63 to 127 days against 30 to 60 days standard. Receivables released SEK 677m in 2025. Those releases do not repeat; I assume working capital returns to about 13.3% of revenue.

Insight for the Consumer. The below-EBITDA lines are a mild positive surprise relative to the last four years and relative to some broker models (interest in particular). They cannot rescue EPS if EBITDA disappoints, because together they are worth about SEK 0.5 of EPS, against SEK 0.31 of EPS for each SEK 100m of EBITDA. The cash story is "self-funding with no surplus": free cash flow covers a 50% payout and Gävle, and leverage drifts down, but there is no visible capacity for buybacks without deferring Gävle.

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?F207th percentilefull answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F5718th percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: there is plausible scope for about SEK 0.5bn to 1.0bn of EBITDA improvement over 2027 to 2029 beyond cost cuts, in a neutral market. It would come mostly from a few big areas, plus many small ones. It is congruent with the stated strategy, but it does not reach SEK 6bn without a favourable wood and price cycle.

The few big areas:

  1. Mix and price in scarce grades (LPB repricing, sack, kraftliner, semichemical fluting). A 5% to 10% LPB price move alone is worth SEK 0.4bn to 0.8bn, but it is contract-lagged.
  2. The North American packaging ramp. 300kt by 2030 against about 44kt annualised now, offsetting graphic decline and lifting utilisation from about 75% to above 90%.
  3. European industry capacity closures (an external event), which would lift operating rates and pricing.

The many small ones: OEE (+2 points a year, about SEK 200m), New Flute and PharmaKraft mix, ERP savings, working capital, and lower capex after 2027.

Peer precedents:

  • Holmen's value-over-volume model sustains EBIT margins of about 14%, but on its own forest.
  • Smurfit Westrock's North American closures restored pricing.
  • Mayr-Melnhof's Fit-For-Future programme delivered EUR 70m in its first year.
  • Stora Enso has achieved EUR 850m of savings since 2023, but its margins are still low.
  • Metsä Board's EUR 200m programme is judged "optimistic" without supportive markets.

So self-help is achievable, while margin targets usually need the market to help. Congruence: these levers match "Way Forward" (profit over volume, the North American Evolution programme, value-based products). The mid-term output in a neutral cycle is about SEK 5.0bn to 5.5bn of EBITDA.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

D4027th percentile

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: over twelve months the revenue engine decelerated sharply and ran well below normal, but it bottomed in Q1 2026 and has turned. The earnings engine (the spread) is accelerating now. The input force behind it (Nordic wood relief) is about to slow, while the price force is strengthening. The net risk has moved from the revenue line to the cost line for 2027.

Revenue on FactSet's figures:

  • The last twelve months to Q3 2026 were about SEK 38.9bn, against SEK 42.7bn a year earlier (-9%).
  • FY2025 was -6.7%. Quarterly year-on-year growth ran -19.5%, -13.8%, -3.5% and +2.9%.
  • "Normal" is hard to define. Before Verso, revenue grew about 1% to 3% a year (FY2019 to FY2021). Since Verso, organic growth has averaged slightly negative (-7%, +6%, -3%).

The deceleration came from European volumes (down 7% to 16% at points in 2025), lower European prices and krona strength (a SEK 1.6bn translation hit in 2025). The input force:

  • Swedish wood relief worked through the P&L during H2 2026 and is now plateauing (Q4 guided flat, 2027 guided flat on average).
  • Price has re-accelerated (about +1% a quarter into H1 2027, with kraftliner and sack rising).

So the engine is re-accelerating on price, with no tailwind from volume or wood in 2027. For the model: revenue growth of about +3% to +4% in 2027 (consensus +4.2%), mostly price, then about +2% to +3% in 2028.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

F3819th percentile

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: consensus forecasts a return to roughly Billerud's own historical average margins by FY2027 to FY2028, from a trough in FY2026. That is credible in direction and somewhat optimistic in level, by about 5% to 10% on FY2027 EBITDA and more on EPS, because the history average included surplus carbon allowance income and a weaker krona, and was earned before the Russian wood shock fully hit. Line by line:

  • Revenue. Consensus -1.5% (FY2026), +4.2%, +3.2%, +4.5%, against organic -7%, +6% and -3% in FY2023 to FY2025 and pre-Verso growth of about 1% to 3%. This needs about 2% to 3% a year from price plus 1% to 2% from volume. It is plausible for 2027; 2028 depends on the virgin premium surviving recycled supply.
  • EBITDA margin (brokers' basis). 8.7%, 11.8%, 12.3%, against 12.3% (FY2024) and 10.1% (FY2025). FactSet operating history averaged about 12% over FY2019 to FY2025. A return to the average is plausible if self-help (SEK 2.3bn) offsets structurally higher wood and lost allowance income.
  • EBITDA in absolute terms. SEK 3.46bn, 4.92bn and 5.26bn. The FY2027 increase of SEK 1.47bn is the largest single-year step since 2022. Visible brokers range from SEK 4.52bn to 5.18bn. My central estimate is SEK 4.5bn to 4.7bn.
  • D&A is flat at about SEK 2.87bn, which is credible. EBIT is SEK 0.6bn, 2.1bn and 2.26bn, a margin of 5.1% to 5.3%, against 5.0% (FY2024) and a FY2021 to FY2025 average of about 5.8%.
  • Interest of about SEK 0.3bn and tax of about 22% to 22.4% are credible.
  • Share count is flat at about 248.7m, which is credible (no buybacks).
  • EPS. 1.32, 5.63 and 6.51, against 6.24 (FY2024) and 2.88 (FY2025). FY2029 (9.10) rests on one broker; ignore it.
  • Capex. SEK 2.50bn, 2.60bn and 2.65bn, credible but excluding most of Gävle (above SEK 3bn from about 2028).
  • Free cash flow. SEK 0.68bn, 1.43bn and 1.42bn, against history of -1.7bn to +0.2bn in FY2023 to FY2025. Credible only if working capital does not rebuild.
  • DPS. SEK 2.00, 2.62 and 3.12. Credible at a 50% payout.

The credibility gap: the change forces show no wood tailwind in 2027 (management's guidance) and rising risk of price give-back and krona strength. Consensus therefore relies on price retention plus self-help.

P-04After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?B5278th percentilefull answer under 5a Pricing power
P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D3827th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the net impact is a return to roughly the post-Verso average excluding the 2022 peak, plus about SEK 0.5bn to 1.0bn of self-help and North American gains, minus about SEK 0.5bn of carbon allowance income that does not return. Revenue stays in the same SEK 40bn to 44bn band as FY2022 to FY2025 (no growth beyond price and 1% to 2% volume). EBITDA averages about SEK 4.9bn over FY2027 to FY2030, about 16% above the FY2023 to FY2025 average (SEK 4.2bn, FactSet operating) but below the FY2022 to FY2025 average including the peak (SEK 5.1bn) and well below the SEK 6bn target. EPS averages about SEK 5.5 over FY2027 to FY2030 against SEK 3.9 for FY2023 to FY2025. Returns stay below the cost of capital throughout (ROCE about 5% in 2027 rising to about 7% in 2030, against an 11% target).

What drives the net change from FY2026 to FY2027 (the largest step), SEKm, adjusted EBITDA.

Incremental savings +250 High

Diffuse in components, concentrated in uncertainty. The bridge has ten items, but three of them (price, wood and the krona) carry about 80% of the variance. The controllable items (savings, maintenance, OEE) add about SEK 0.5bn with reasonable confidence; that alone takes EBITDA from SEK 3.4bn to about SEK 3.9bn, which is a sensible floor for FY2027 unless volume collapses. Everything above SEK 3.9bn depends on price exceeding inflation and wood not rising before H2 2027.

Why my numbers are below consensus, quantified for FY2027 (consensus SEK 4.92bn, mine SEK 4.5bn). About SEK 0.25bn to 0.3bn from the wood reading (consensus appears to assume most of the H2 2026 wood level persists; DNB Carnegie expects "significant declines in Scandinavian wood costs to support earnings through 2027"); about SEK 0.1bn to 0.15bn from price retention (LPB annual round); about SEK 0.05bn from currency. FactSet's median sits above four of the five named brokers, so part of the gap is a consensus artefact (Nordea at SEK 5,181m and the restricted broker at SEK 4,959m pull it up).

FY2028 onward. The step is small (+4% EBITDA in 2028), because the wood round trip and recycled supply absorb the North American ramp, OEE and volume. FY2029 to FY2030 assume a neutral cycle: margins of 11.6% to 12.0% and EBITDA of SEK 5.0bn to 5.3bn, consistent with the first set's "neutral-cycle" mid-term potential of SEK 5.0bn to 5.5bn.

Insight for the Consumer. The forecast does not describe a new earnings level for Billerud; it describes the old level after the carbon allowance income has gone and the cost programmes have been banked. That is why the EPS path (SEK 4.4 to 6.5) sits far below the FY2029 single-broker figure of SEK 9.10 and the SEK 6bn target, and why returns stay below the cost of capital. Any investment case that needs more than that needs a supply-side event (European closures) that is not in any dated plan.

Forecasts and upgrades

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

F3016th percentile

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Judgement: the gap between our assessment and what guidance, estimates and the narrative reflect is unfavourable on balance, and it is unfavourable in a particular way. Nothing important is unknown to the market. The difference is that the price and the consensus numbers have adopted the favourable reading of known facts, and we have not. Our FY2027 EBITDA is 9% below consensus and our EPS 22% below; our FY2028 EBITDA is below every named broker. Guidance offers no lever to close the gap upward. The gap is also unevenly spread in time: the next two reports are likely to look strong and need not disturb consensus, and the test comes from July 2027.

The three answers together.

  • Guidance (I-01). There is no annual earnings guidance to raise. The controllable items will be met or slightly beaten, worth SEK 50m to 100m. The dividend is likely to come in below consensus. The mid-term targets are more likely to be re-based than raised.
  • Estimates (I-02). Consensus asks for the second-half 2026 run-rate plus 12%. We forecast the run-rate plus 3%. Cuts are more likely than upgrades over 18 months (about 60% against 20% for FY2027), but they come late. Cash flow estimates face a dated cut when Gävle is decided.
  • Narrative (I-03). The positives are known and priced. The negatives are known to specialists and absent from the numbers. Three positives are not priced (a closure, a flat-to-higher LPB price, a tight US board market), against three negatives that are under-weighted (wood in the second half of 2027, Gävle spending, and the fading of estimate momentum).

What this means for the valuation the memo will present. The shares trade on 13.6x two-year-forward earnings on consensus, at the 91st percentile of five years. On the model's earnings the same price is 18.0x. The model's return at the guideline fair multiple of 15.5x, which coincides with the 90th percentile of the stock's ten-year range in the financials block, is +9.0% to 31 March 2028 and +22.8% to 30 September 2029. The 18-month figure should be about +6.6% once the dividend timing is corrected (D-01). All of the return comes from earnings growth (+21.5% and +34.3%) and dividends. The multiple subtracts 13.7%. So even when the stock is granted a multiple near the top of its own history, our earnings support a return of about 5% to 7.5% a year. The reason is the gap described above: the market's multiple is applied to consensus earnings that we do not expect to be earned.

The asymmetry in time, which is the most useful steer I can give. For roughly the next five months the visible evidence should favour the shares: Q4 2026 EBITDA about 40% above a year earlier, Q1 2027 more than double, a positive first-quarter pricing outlook, possibly a flat LPB round, and an estimate-momentum rating that stays high until its window rolls. None of that tests our disagreement with consensus. From the Q2 2027 report onward the evidence turns to wood after salvage, the virgin premium after the UK start-ups, second-half pricing and the Gävle decision, and on each of those our view is less favourable than what is reflected. The memo should not read early confirmation as evidence that consensus is right.

What would make the gap favourable. A European capacity closure by Billerud or a peer. Wood staying at its late-2026 low through 2027 together with a higher LPB price, in which case consensus is right and we are too low. Or a lower share price: the first set put a fair trough at SEK 58 to 67, near book-value support, and at such levels the same forecasts would sit above what the price reflects.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F4020th percentile

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: Billerud gives no annual earnings guidance, so there is nothing of that kind to raise, and the usual chain from a guidance upgrade to estimate upgrades does not exist for this stock. What it does guide (next-quarter blocks, savings, capex, the wood statement and the dividend) is most likely to be met at the next two reports, with one probable disappointment against consensus: the dividend. From mid-2027 the informal guidance that matters most, on wood and on second-half pricing, is more likely to turn more cautious than more confident. Over 18 months to three years the mid-term targets are far more likely to be kept or re-based than raised. Guidance will not be a source of positive surprise. The gap between our view and what guidance reflects is neutral to slightly unfavourable.

The events. Six reports fall in the 18 months: Q4 2026 (2 February 2027), Q1 2027 (late April), Q2 2027 (mid-July), Q3 2027 (late October), Q4 2027 (early February 2028) and Q1 2028 (late April). A capital markets day is plausible in late 2027 or 2028: the last was in December 2024, the Evolution programme completes in 2027 and the Gävle decision is due in 2027.

Each guided item, with my probabilities.

Guided item Raised or better Met or repeated Reduced or worse What drives it
Q4 2026 blocks (maintenance SEK 80m, input costs about SEK 60m higher, price about +1%, wood flat) about 80% about 20% Seasonal European volume; the Frövi shutdown.
Savings of SEK 800m from 2027 about 10% about 85% about 5% The 2026 target was raised from SEK 500m to 550m. All staff reductions are done.
2027 wood "stable on average against 2026", on 2 February 2027 about 15% about 70% about 15% First-half price lists are stable; management has no reason to change the wording yet.
The same wood statement by the July and October 2027 reports about 15% about 50% about 35% Spring and autumn price lists once salvage supply has gone.
Pricing outlook for Q1 2027, given on 2 February about 60% positive about 30% flat about 10% negative Kraftliner (+EUR 80 a tonne) and sack (+EUR 50) rises reaching contracts; the LPB round.
Pricing outlook for the second half of 2027, given in July about 25% positive about 45% flat about 30% negative UK recycled capacity; cartonboard; whether wood is rising, which supports price.
2027 capex of SEK 2.6bn about 25% cut about 50% about 25% raised It was cut in both 2025 and 2026 on phasing. It rises if Gävle is approved in 2027 with first payments: Frövi took SEK 0.4bn of its SEK 2.6bn in the year of decision.
Dividend for FY2026, against SEK 2.00 in consensus about 5% above about 30% at SEK 2.00 about 65% below See below.
Carbon "pretty neutral" in 2027 about 85% about 15% The Riksdag vote is not scheduled; allowance prices.
300kt of North American packaging by 2030 about 75% repeated about 25% softened "Aggressive volume growth from 2027" is needed; Somerset adds supply.

The dividend. I agree with the second set's SEK 1.50 and keep the input, with a wide range (SEK 1.00 to 2.00). The policy is at least 50% of net profit. Adjusted EPS for 2026 will be about SEK 1.1 to 1.4 and reported EPS about SEK 0.9, so SEK 2.00 would be a payout well above 100%. This board cut the 2025 dividend to SEK 2.00 from SEK 3.50 when consensus expected SEK 3.30 to 3.50, and the Gävle decision is pending. The case for holding SEK 2.00 is that it is affordable (free cash flow of about SEK 0.7bn in 2026 against a cost of SEK 0.5bn; leverage falling) and that a second cut would sit oddly with a recovery. I give that about 30%.

Gävle as a guidance event. The company's "above SEK 3bn" dates from December 2024. Frövi, a 500kt mill, cost SEK 2.6bn in 2021 to 2023. Gävle is a 755kt to 775kt mill with two old boilers, the supplier market is a duopoly, and construction costs have risen since. I judge that a figure of SEK 3.5bn or more is about as likely as not when the decision is announced, unless the company chooses the cheaper "intermediate solution" it has said it is studying. The second set's SEK 3.1bn is at the low end. I leave the capex inputs unchanged because the amount and phasing are unknowable until the decision, but the Consumer should treat capex for 2028 to 2030 as carrying upside risk of SEK 0.1bn to 0.3bn a year.

The company's guidance style, and what it means for the memo. Management is conservative on what it controls and silent on what it does not. "Conservative guidance" is therefore true only of the controllable items, which add about SEK 0.45bn in 2027. Over-delivery there might be worth SEK 50m to 100m. That is small against a single per cent on price (SEK 0.4bn). One signal deserves attention: Billerud launched its cost programmes in 2023 and September 2025, both times as the spread deteriorated. A new programme in the second half of 2027 would be a raise in self-help guidance and a warning about the spread at the same time.

Secondarily, the mid-term targets (EBITDA above SEK 6bn over a cycle, margin above 15%, ROCE above 11%, Region Europe SEK 4.3bn to 4.8bn, Region North America SEK 2.0bn to 2.5bn). Over 18 months to three years: raised, under 5%; unchanged, about 65%; reduced or re-based, about 30%.

  • Why not raised. On our forecast EBITDA reaches SEK 5.3bn in 2030 and Region Europe about SEK 3.7bn, against a range that starts at SEK 4.3bn. Jefferies calls the European range unachievable under current cost structures.
  • Why mostly unchanged. "Over a cycle" has no date, so the targets cannot formally fail. The North American range is within reach.
  • Why possibly re-based. The precedent is December 2024, when the margin target was cut from 17% to 15% for structurally higher wood. A capital markets day with Gävle approved, a wood rebound and no closure would be the occasion. A re-basing could be presented constructively, for example a lower European range and a higher North American one.
  • What would make the targets met in fact. A European closure together with wood relief. Neither is in a dated plan.

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: consensus for FY2027 and FY2028 is more likely to be cut than raised over the next 18 months, but probably not at the next report. I expect FY2027 consensus to hold, give or take 2%, through the Q4 2026 report on 2 February 2027, because the first half of 2027 is not where we differ from consensus, and because the brokers at the bottom of the range may have to raise their numbers if the LPB price is flat. The cuts I expect begin with the Q2 2027 report in July 2027, when second-half wood and prices come into view. Over the whole period I put the chance that FY2027 consensus EBITDA ends lower than it is today at about 60%, within 2% at about 20%, and higher at about 20%. A 9% cut to EBITDA is a 22% cut to EPS. The dashboard's sell-side momentum rating of A is very likely to fade within three to six months even without cuts, because the upgrades that produced it will leave the measurement window.

Among the five named brokers our FY2027 EBITDA is at the very bottom (Pareto 4,519; Jefferies 4,532; DNB Carnegie 4,649; one unnamed broker 4,959; Nordea 5,181). Our FY2028 EBITDA is below all five (the lowest is Jefferies at 4,881).

I disagree with the first set on where the centre of expectations lies. The first set took the median of the five named brokers (SEK 4.65bn) as the true centre and treated FactSet's higher figure as an artefact. FactSet's mean of 4,995 cannot be produced from the five named brokers, whose mean is 4,768. There must be further contributors, and they must average well above SEK 5bn. Jefferies itself cites a Bloomberg consensus of SEK 4.8bn for 2027 (23 October 2026). The number against which results and outlooks will be judged is therefore SEK 4.8bn to 5.0bn. Our gap to it is 9% to 10%, not 3%.

Where in the year the gap lies. If consensus is spread evenly it expects about SEK 2.45bn in each half of 2027. Our first half is SEK 2.25bn to 2.45bn (the second set's path at the low end, my run-rate rebuild at the high end): between level and 8% below. Our second half is about SEK 2.25bn: 8% to 10% below. Put differently, consensus needs the second-half 2026 run-rate plus 12%, and we forecast the run-rate plus 3% (D-01). The first two reports of 2027 cannot settle that.

The likely path, report by report (FY2027 EBITDA consensus; then FY2028).

Report Up by more than 1% Within 1% Down by more than 1% Reasoning
Q4 2026 (2 February 2027) about 30% about 40% about 30% Consensus implies about SEK 1.24bn for Q4; ours is SEK 1.16bn. Q4 has missed in two of the last three years. Net breadth of revisions. Balanced to slightly positive until February, because FY2026 and Q4 are being finalised and the low-end brokers have room to rise. Negative from the spring if our path is right, led by the brokers above SEK 4.9bn.

Why the A rating should fade even without cuts. FY2027 EPS has been revised up 10% in one month, 20% in three and 25% in six. Those rises came after the Q2 and Q3 reports, the latest between 22 October and 5 November. By early February they leave the three-month window. Unless FY2027 EPS is raised by a similar amount again, which would take it above every named broker, the three-month reading falls towards zero. The quality of the upgrades points the same way: EPS +20% in three months rests on EBITDA +6% and sales +1%. It is leverage on a small change. The trough-exit upgrades are also complete: FY2026 EPS has already moved from a low of SEK 0.41 to SEK 1.26.

What each outcome needs.

  • Upgrades, to SEK 5.0bn or more for FY2027. An LPB price up 2% to 3% (about +SEK 0.2bn), wood at the Q4 2026 level through all of 2027 (about +SEK 0.2bn to 0.3bn against our path), and EUR/SEK at or above 11 with a firm dollar (about +SEK 0.1bn). Consensus needs most of these together. A closure announcement would lift FY2028, not FY2027.
  • Held. The first half delivered as we expect, and mixed evidence on the second.
  • Cuts. Higher spring wood price lists, an LPB price cut, EUR/SEK towards 10.5, a Q1 miss, or cartonboard prices given back in the second half.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. FY2028 consensus needs a further 7% on top of an FY2027 figure we already think is too high. I put the chance of a cut at about 60%, held at about 25% and raised at about 15%. There is also a specific, dated reason for cuts to cash flow estimates. Consensus capex is SEK 2,651m for FY2028 and SEK 2,452m for FY2029 (one broker), which leaves almost nothing for a Gävle boiler of more than SEK 3bn to be built in 2028 to 2030. When the decision is announced in 2027, capex estimates should rise by SEK 0.4bn to 1.2bn a year, and free cash flow and dividend estimates (SEK 3.12 for FY2028; SEK 6.25 for FY2029 from one broker) should fall. FY2029 (EBITDA 6,082; EPS 9.10) rests on a single broker and should be disregarded.

Current multiple

D3219th percentileMemo topic 11, The fair multiple: the Rater's score1 answer
D-0748
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C-4853rd percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: day to day, a little under half of Billerud's share price movement is shared with its sector. Over the six months as a whole, almost none of its return was. The latest rolling six-month window shows a correlation of 0.66 with the peer basket, a beta of 1.07 and 44% of daily variance explained, near the top of the 24-month range of 20% to 47%. Yet the stock returned +39% in that window while the basket returned -8%. The multiple has moved on its own in the same way: Billerud's forward P/E is at the 91st percentile of its five-year range while most peers are in the bottom third of theirs. The multiple is therefore company-driven. It rests on one company-specific fact, that Billerud is the only stock in its sector with strongly rising estimates, and on the market's willingness to look through 2027 to a normal year. That makes the multiple more exposed to our forecast being right than a sector-driven multiple would be.

The measurements (rolling 126 trading days to 23 November 2026).

Measure Latest 24-month range of month-end readings
Correlation with the equal-weighted basket of 15 peers 0.66 0.45 to 0.68
Beta to the basket 1.07 0.79 to 1.29
Share of daily variance explained by the basket 44% 20% to 47%
The company's own share 56% 53% to 80%
Six-month return: Billerud against the basket +39.3% against -7.8%

The closest individual links are Stora Enso (correlation 0.57), Mondi (0.54) and Metsä Board (0.53). The basket has no price history for SCA and Holmen, the two nearest Swedish comparators, and includes glass, cork and textile-fibre companies. The shared portion against true peers is therefore probably somewhat higher than 44%, perhaps about half.

What the price data say.

  • Daily moves are shared; the trend was not. A beta of 1.07 applied to the basket's -7.8% implies about -8% for Billerud. It rose 39%. Some 47 points of return in six months were the company's own.
  • Beta has been at or above 1.0 since mid-2025. In a sector sell-off the stock falls at least as far as the sector.
  • The shared portion has risen in the last two months (34% at end-September, 46% at end-October, 44% now). That fits a market regime that is risk-off beneath the index: sector and macro news are taking a larger share of the daily move.

What the multiple data say.

  • Billerud's 24-month forward P/E is 13.6x against a five-year median of 11.3x. Its current P/E is at the 65th percentile of its own ten-year history. The same measure for peers: Mayr-Melnhof 0, Stora Enso 10th, Holmen 10th, Metsä Board 21st, UPM 35th, SCA 55th, Mondi 77th.
  • The dashboard's sell-side momentum letter is A for Billerud, B for SIG, and C to F for every other stock in the sector. Within this sector the multiple has followed estimate revisions, company by company.
  • A point the earlier sets did not make. The share price is up 43% in six months but only 4.6% in twelve, while FY2027 consensus EPS is about 30% lower than twelve months ago. The P/E on FY2027 earnings has therefore risen from about 10.5x to about 15.3x in a year. The market has not simply followed estimates upward. It has chosen to value the company on a normal year beyond 2027 (the 24-month forward EPS in consensus is SEK 6.62) and on book value (about 0.8x, from a low of 0.55x).

What this means for the Consumer.

  • With more than half of the variance its own, and the whole of the recent re-rating its own, the shares will respond to Billerud's delivery. That is the favourable reading.
  • The premium has no sector support beneath it. If Billerud's estimates stop rising, the natural reference becomes peers that are trading at the low end of their own ranges on falling estimates.
  • The multiple embeds SEK 6.5 or more of EPS two years out. Our FY2028 EPS is SEK 5.1. On the model's own earnings the two-year-forward P/E is 18.0x, not 13.6x.
  • Sector events will still move the stock on the day: Stora Enso's forest listing, the January reports from SCA and Holmen, the kraftliner index, and Mondi's trading updates.
11h

Estimated TSR [to nearest 5% band]

D3927th percentileMemo topic 12, Total shareholder return: the Rater's score

Also under this rating, as market figures rather than answers b Historical range; c Peers historical range; e Peers' average current multiple; f Current multiple percentile; g Peers' average current percentile. They are shown on the ratings map.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

F4020th percentile

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement: Billerud fits the classic early-recovery pattern of a cyclical non-integrated mill, and the stock has already captured the first, multiple-led leg. The second leg historically needs earnings delivery and a supply-side event, and the evidence for both is incomplete.

Patterns of outperformance in this sector:

  1. Buying at trough book value when the price-cost spread inflects. Billerud hit an all-time low of about 0.55x P/B in mid-2026. The shares have risen from about SEK 58 to 61 to SEK 89 (+43% in six months, the 81st percentile of the universe) as the spread turned.
  2. High P/E at the trough is a buy signal; low P/E at the peak is a sell signal. The 24-month forward P/E is 13.6x, the 91st percentile of five years, on depressed earnings. This is consistent with the pattern, not a warning in itself.
  3. Owning the scarce grade. A mill that sells paper keeps more of a price rise than a group that sells boxes, and scarcity moves profit towards the producers of the scarce product. Billerud fits: it is the purest exposure to fresh-fibre scarcity in the sector.
  4. Supply exit. North America re-rated on closures. Billerud is mooting European closures but has not acted.
  5. Forest crystallisation. This does not apply: Billerud owns no forest.

Patterns of underperformance:

  1. Capital-heavy conversions in a downturn (Stora Enso Oulu, Norske Skog Golbey, Mondi growth capex). Billerud avoided this by cancelling Escanaba, which is a point in its favour.
  2. Non-integrated buyers in a wood upcycle. Billerud fits this pattern badly: 2022 to 2025.
  3. Leverage in a trough. Billerud is moderate at 2.0x.

Wisdom Library rules to apply: "competitive intensity is entry minus exit" (European exits remain slow), "pricing power breaks before demand does", and "lazy cyclical extrapolation". In sum, Billerud is a cyclical recovery that has been rerated from trough. Further outperformance needs 2027 EBITDA delivery near consensus and evidence that wood relief and price gains are retained. History says the second leg is harder.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

D4432nd percentile

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: from SEK 89, a fall to a fair trough valuation would take the shares to about SEK 58 to 67 (-25% to -35%), with a tail to about SEK 45 to 50 (-45% to -50%) in a joint wood, currency and price shock. Three anchors:

  1. Price to book. Consensus book value is about SEK 111 a share for FY2026. The all-time trough was about 0.55x, reached in mid-2026, with a 52-week low of SEK 58 to 61. At 0.55x to 0.6x book the price is SEK 61 to 67. Book value itself would fall with translation losses if the krona strengthened (OCI translation was -SEK 1.5bn in 2025).
  2. EV/EBITDA on trough EBITDA. Net debt is about SEK 5.5bn to 6.0bn, with about 250m diluted shares. - A repeat of 2026 EBITDA (about SEK 3.4bn) at a trough multiple of 6.0x gives an EV of SEK 20.4bn and equity of SEK 14.4bn to 14.9bn: about SEK 58 to 60. - The Jefferies bear case (EBITDA of SEK 3.0bn at 5.5x to 6.25x) gives SEK 42 to 51. The Risks report's bear target is SEK 50.
  3. P/E. The 10th percentile of the five-year 24-month forward P/E is 9.75x. On a cut forward EPS of about SEK 5.5 to 6.0, that gives about SEK 54 to 59.

For comparison, the upside references are the mean target of SEK 97.5 (+9%), the DNB Carnegie bull case of SEK 140, and the Jefferies valuation of 6.5x on SEK 4.5bn of EBITDA (about SEK 93). The risk-reward is asymmetric to the downside at today's price, unless FY2027 EBITDA is delivered at or above consensus.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

F3617th percentile

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative today is "Billerud is the purest way to own scarce fresh-fibre kraft paper and lower Swedish wood". It has driven a +43% six-month re-rating to the 91st percentile of five-year forward P/E. The next six months offer well-founded but expected confirmations: Q4 wood and price, the savings run-rate, ETS1 re-entry. The narrative's first dated threats are UK recycled capacity in March 2027, the end of salvage wood supply, and the Q2 2027 hedge roll-off. Genuinely novel positive steps (European capacity closure, consolidation, buybacks) are possible but undated. Unless one of them is announced, the narrative is likely to move from "recovery" to "how much of the recovery sticks" during 2027, and that question is usually priced at lower multiples.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–60

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the two earlier sets are congruent in everything that sets the shape of the case, and they jar in six specific places. Three of the six alter the shape of 2027 without altering its level. After correcting them I still arrive at FY2027 EBITDA of about SEK 4.5bn, so the base case survives the test. The corrections matter most for the claim that three adverse forces "arrive together in the second half of 2027": on the evidence, two dated forces arrive then (wood and UK recycled capacity), not three.

What aligns. Both sets describe a spread business whose 2026 trough is being left through cost and price, not demand. Both put FY2027 EBITDA at about SEK 4.5bn to 4.7bn, below consensus. Both find the controllable items dependable and the market items not. Both find a strong balance sheet that is not being used for returns, and returns on capital below the cost of capital to 2030. The first set's scores (near-term external forces positive, competitive advantage and economic strength weak, risks high) and the second set's (43 overall) tell one story. I agree with that story.

Where the sets jar with each other or with the evidence, and what I conclude.

  1. The currency hedges (both sets against the company's own table). The earlier sets state that euro flows are 80% hedged in Q1 2027 and 38% in Q2 2027 at about SEK 10.97, and treat the fall as a hedge roll-off in mid-2027. Two AlphaSense reports cite the Q3 2026 interim report differently and consistently with each other: euro flows 80% hedged for Q4 2026 at SEK 11.02, 73% for Q1 2027 at SEK 10.77, 50% for Q2, 35% for Q3 and nil for Q4 2027; US dollar flows 70% at SEK 9.36, 69% at SEK 9.38, 49% at SEK 9.45, 34% and nil. The outstanding hedges had a negative market value of SEK 135m at 30 September 2026. Policy allows 0% to 80% of net flows to be hedged over a rolling 15 months. The Consumer should check the figures against the report itself. Three conclusions follow. - The declining percentages are how a rolling programme always looks at a quarter-end. Q4 2027 shows nil only because it had just entered the window. By the time each quarter arrives it will be largely hedged, at the forward rates prevailing over the preceding year. Protection does not "fall away" at a date. - The effect of the krona therefore reaches profit smoothed and with a lag of roughly two to four quarters. A krona move in the first half of 2027 sets the rates for the second half of 2027 and the first half of 2028. - The negative market value shows that spot rates were above the hedge rates at 30 September 2026. The first half of 2027 is thus locked at rates below today's spot (a known, modest drag: the euro rate falls from SEK 11.02 to SEK 10.77), and if spot merely holds, later quarters convert at better rates than the first half. On my estimate, if rates hold near their levels of 30 September 2026 the krona costs about SEK 50m in 2027 against 2026, not the SEK 150m in the second set's bridge. If the krona strengthens as ING (EUR/SEK 10.80) and Morgan Stanley (10.40 by end-2027) expect, the cost falls mainly on 2028.
  2. Carbon (first set: +SEK 0.1bn to 0.3bn in 2027; second set: +SEK 100m). The research on the Swedish proposal shows the 2026 carbon tax costs about SEK 100m for the year (SEK 57m was booked in Q3, of which SEK 47m related to the first half). Re-entry to the EU scheme from 1 January 2027 ends the tax but brings no free allowances, and the chief financial officer said the year-on-year effect "should be pretty neutral". The earlier retroactive draft was dropped. The Riksdag vote is not yet scheduled. I therefore put carbon at nil in the 2027 bridge. The risk is now two-sided and small: allowance prices ranged from EUR 62 to EUR 91 a tonne in early 2026.
  3. LPB (first set: "multi-year contracts, the one high-visibility leg"; second set: annual pricing, price down 1% to 3%). The second set is right on structure. The chief executive confirmed on 30 January 2026 that all LPB volume is on one-year price terms, and Billerud took price declines in the 2026 round while Stora Enso, on two- to four-year agreements, achieved slightly higher prices. On direction for 2027 the evidence is better than the second set assumed. The Nippon Dynawave mill (about 8% of global LPB capacity) has been out since 26 May 2026; only a phased restart of some operations on bought-in pulp is planned for December, with no date for full capacity and a regulator's order to rebuild or drain other tanks. Jefferies' model for SIG, a buyer, assumes about 3% LPB cost inflation in 2027. Elopak has passed board costs on through surcharges. Against this, wood costs have fallen and buyers know it. My central case is a flat LPB price (range -3% to +3%, worth about SEK 0.25bn either way). That adds about SEK 100m to the second set's price line.
  4. How to read the wood guidance (first set: flat from the exit rate would be upside; second set: a middle reading worth +SEK 200m). The research supports a reading slightly closer to management's literal words. Jefferies reads "stable on average against 2026" as a step up from the Q4 2026 level. SCA reported no further wood price decreases going into Q1 2027. Salvage supply from Storm Johannes dries up in late 2026 and early 2027. After the comparable storms of 2013 and 2014 pulpwood fell only 8% and the rebound was modest. With the three- to six-month lag, wood cost in the accounts is flat at its low through the first half of 2027 and rises from about Q3. I put the 2027 benefit at +SEK 150m (the second set: +200).
  5. Two items the second set classed as controllable that the first set did not. The first set told the modeller to assume a recurring SEK 50m to 100m a year of overrun risk at North American shutdowns. The second set then credited +SEK 100m for the non-recurrence of the 2026 overruns. The 2027 Escanaba shutdown also carries the final Evolution installations. I credit +SEK 50m. Likewise I credit +SEK 50m, not +100m, for equipment effectiveness (OEE), because no achieved figure has ever been disclosed.
  6. "Reported 2026 net profit close to zero" (second set). FactSet's reported net income for the first nine months is SEK -71m (-219, -64, +212), and Q4 should add roughly SEK 0.3bn. Reported FY2026 net profit is therefore more likely about SEK 0.2bn (about SEK 0.9 a share) than zero. The second set's dividend conclusion (below the SEK 2.00 in consensus) still stands, but on policy and prudence, not on a zero profit (I-01).

Two smaller points. Region Europe's Q1 2026 EBITDA was SEK 121m (a 2% margin, Jefferies), not the SEK 245m the second set estimated; the group total is unaffected. And the first set's tripwire for the Q4 2026 report (a Region Europe margin below 10%) is too loose: the guided blocks imply about 13% (D-05).

The amended FY2027 bridge (SEKm). FY2026 3,378; savings +250; maintenance +100; North American non-recurrence +50; OEE +50; wood +150; price +850; non-wood and wage inflation -425; krona -50; carbon 0; volume +100. Total about 4,450. The second set's bridge gives 4,494. The difference is one-tenth of a margin point, which is below the resolution of the judgement.

A second, independent rebuild from the run-rate. Second-half 2026 EBITDA is about SEK 2.19bn (Q3 1,032 plus Q4 about 1,160), or SEK 4.4bn annualised, with maintenance at a normal annual rate. Carrying that into 2027 and adding the savings step-up, the price already announced net of cost inflation, wage rises, a small seasonal penalty for the first half, and rising wood in the second half gives about SEK 2.3bn for the first half and SEK 2.25bn to 2.3bn for the second: about SEK 4.6bn. Two insights come from this rebuild.

  • The first set's run-rate method and the second set's bridge method agree to within SEK 0.15bn. The apparent conflict between them (the second set charged the first with counting wood twice) is small once Q1 2026 is recognised as an abnormally weak quarter. On my estimate, its Region Europe margin of 2% left EBITDA about SEK 0.3bn below what the disclosed blocks explain.
  • Our FY2027 is the second-half 2026 run-rate plus about 3%. FactSet's consensus is that run-rate plus about 12%. The recovery to SEK 4.4bn has already happened. What separates us from consensus is whether a further SEK 0.5bn arrives on top of it.

The two cross-check differences the model summary flags.

  • FY2026 EPS: 1.09 (cross-check) against 1.19 (model). Pre-tax profit is identical in both (SEK 348m). The whole difference is tax. The cross-check taxes the year at 22%. The model uses the tax actually recorded for the three reported quarters, which is lower, so its tax for the year is SEK 53m, an effective 15%. No input is wrong. For comparison with consensus (1.32), 1.09 is the like-for-like figure.
  • FY2026 free cash flow: 710 against 956. About SEK 200m is the cash cost of items affecting comparability (mainly the 2025 restructuring provision paid out in 2026), which the Analyst deducted and for which the inputs have no line. The remaining SEK 45m or so is the same tax difference. No input is wrong. FY2027 to FY2030 agree to within SEK 4m.
  • The summary's phasing note (Q4 2026 margin moved by +3.1 points) has the same root. The three reported quarters are on FactSet's operating basis (EBITDA 1,914), while the year's input is on the adjusted basis (3,378). The model therefore shows a Q4 2026 EBITDA of about 1,464. The true expectation is about 1,160 adjusted. The year is right; the quarter in the model is a balancing figure and should not be quoted.

A point for the Financial Analyst, which matters for the return the memo will quote. The Financial Analyst should check the timing of dividends in the model. The model's net debt falls each year by exactly its equity free cash flow less that same fiscal year's dividend per share times 248.7m shares (FY2027: 948.3 less 534.7 equals the 413.6 change shown; FY2028 to FY2030 reconcile in the same way). The dividend input, however, is the amount declared for the year and paid the following spring. The effect is that the 18-month return counts the FY2027 dividend (SEK 2.15) before 31 March 2028, when it will be paid in about May 2028. "From dividends +4.1%" (SEK 3.65 on SEK 89.15) should be about +1.7% (SEK 1.50), and the 18-month return about +6.6%, or about 4.8% annualised, not +9.0% and 6.5%. The three-year figure is unaffected, because by 30 September 2029 the same three dividends have been paid on either timing. Year-end net debt is overstated by the following spring's dividend (about SEK 0.4bn at end-2026, which is why the model shows SEK 6.1bn against the cross-check's SEK 5.9bn).

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

F3318th percentile

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

Judgement: almost every dynamic in our assessment has been written about by at least one broker, so little of it is unknown. What differs is where each one sits. The favourable dynamics are widely known and are in both the share price and the consensus numbers. The unfavourable ones are known to specialists, are stated plainly by the two Hold-rated brokers, and are not in the consensus numbers. Our base case is close to Jefferies' published view: it is not a new insight, but it is a minority one. Three points are little discussed anywhere: that the hedge table is not a cliff, that consensus contains almost no Gävle spending, and that the estimate upgrades are leverage on small EBITDA changes. The evidence on investor commentary is thin, and the three Buy-rated brokers' reasoning is not visible to us.

The limits of the evidence. The narrative report draws on Jefferies and DNB Carnegie only, both at Hold since 29 September and 1 October 2026. It holds no research from Nordea, Pareto or the third Buy-rated house. Its section on consensus and revisions retrieved material on a different company (BILL Holdings, a US software company) and is void. It contains no investor commentary from social media, fund letters or the press. For (ii) I therefore infer from price and positioning: a rise of 43% in six months, relative strength at the 81st percentile, a forward P/E at the 91st percentile of five years, and 70% of ratings at Buy.

(i) The sell-side narrative. It is split, and the split is visible in the numbers. Two brokers at Hold say in plain terms what we conclude: Jefferies that consensus needs a SEK 1.4bn rise that is "too ambitious" and that further progress needs "recovery or strategic actions", DNB Carnegie that near-term visibility is limited. On the other side, Nordea (SEK 5.2bn) and an unnamed Buy-rated broker (SEK 5.0bn) are high for FY2027, the contributors we cannot see average above SEK 5bn, and Pareto, also at Buy, is low for FY2027 (SEK 4.5bn) but high for FY2028 (SEK 5.4bn). An investor who reads only Jefferies already has most of our case. An investor who reads consensus has none of it.

(ii) The investor narrative, as far as price reveals it. The shares have sided with the favourable reading. The sector note's phrase, that Billerud is the purest way to own scarce fresh-fibre paper and lower Swedish wood, describes a stock that has returned 39% in six months while its peers fell 8%. The P/E on FY2027 earnings has risen from about 10.5x to about 15.3x in twelve months, so investors are paying for a normal year beyond 2027, not for 2027 itself. I cannot say from the evidence how many holders are aware of the second-half risks. The two downgrades were public and specific, so awareness is probably fair among specialists and low among those who own the stock for estimate momentum.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. The narrative here is thin and rests on "normalisation": consensus EPS of SEK 6.5 for FY2028 and a single broker's SEK 9.1 for FY2029, against our SEK 5.1 and 5.9. The SEK 6bn target is doubted in print by Jefferies and contradicted by no one with a bridge. Some hope of buybacks remains in sell-side models (Jefferies has modelled capacity of about SEK 1bn a year if EBITDA approaches SEK 6bn), which our forecast does not support once Gävle is funded. The one mid-term positive that is neither priced nor in numbers is a capacity closure.

What would change the narrative, in order of date. The LPB outcome and the dividend (2 February 2027). The start-up at King's Lynn and the kraftliner index (March to June 2027). The first rise in Swedish wood price lists. The Gävle decision. Any closure or consolidation step. The mechanical fading of the estimate-momentum rating.

Each layer's own material

What each layer was asked, what its scores mean, and the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 20 November 2026, 13:21 to 13:40 UK, Opus, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Billerud (BILL-SE): answers to the first set of questions, as at 20 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer (the frame, the base numbers and the data traps)

Source check. The caution about the latest Earnings Review is resolved: it states that it covers the period ending 30 September 2026, reported on 22 October 2026. This is the last period in the FactSet block (09/2026). The next results are on 2 February 2027.

The frame in one paragraph. Billerud's earnings are a spread, set by four variables that each move group EBITDA by more than any end-market volume change: (1) the price of its virgin-fibre grades (1% of price is about SEK 0.4bn of EBITDA); (2) Nordic wood, which it buys almost entirely from outside (under 5% self-sufficiency, about 10 million cubic metres a year; a 10% change in wood cost moves EBITDA by about SEK 0.9bn); (3) the Swedish krona, because over 80% of revenue is in foreign currency and over 55% of costs are in SEK (a 10% stronger krona removes about SEK 640m before hedges); (4) self-help (SEK 800m of savings by 2027, maintenance timing, and OEE, where 1 point is worth about SEK 100m). 2026 is the trough year. The recovery under way is driven by price and cost, not by demand. The question for the model is how much of the 2026 cost relief survives into 2027 and 2028, and how much is given back through price or currency.

Base numbers to anchor on (brokers' basis, SEKm). FY2025: revenue 40,488; EBITDA 4,098 (10.1%); EPS 2.88. FactSet consensus: FY2026 39,896 / 3,457 (8.7%) / 1.32; FY2027 41,574 / 4,923 (11.8%) / 5.63; FY2028 42,906 / 5,258 (12.3%) / 6.51. FY2029 rests on one broker. The five brokers FactSet names put FY2027 EBITDA at 4,519 to 5,181 (mean 4,768, median 4,649) and FY2027 EPS at 4.24 to 6.61 (median 5.12). FactSet's own median (4,923; EPS 5.63) is therefore above the median of the named brokers. Treat 4.65bn and 5.1 to 5.6 as the true centre of street expectations for FY2027.

2026 by quarter (company adjusted EBITDA, SEKm). Q1 525 (5%), Q2 661 (7%), Q3 1,032 (10%), Q4 about 1,177 (Jefferies estimate). Scheduled maintenance costs were about 184 in Q1 (derived as 1,029 for nine months less Q2 442 and Q3 403), 442 in Q2 and 403 in Q3, with about 80 in Q4 (Frövi). That makes about SEK 1.1bn for 2026, against SEK 782m for the first nine months of 2025. Before maintenance, underlying EBITDA ran at about 709, 1,103, 1,435 and 1,257 in Q1 to Q4.

My central judgement for the model (the Consumer should test it). FY2027 EBITDA of SEK 4.5bn to 4.7bn, with a plausible range of 3.9bn to 5.3bn. The logic: the H2 2026 underlying run-rate (about SEK 5.4bn a year before maintenance), less normal maintenance (about SEK 0.9bn to 1.0bn), plus the incremental SEK 250m of savings, plus price carry-over (about SEK 0.2bn to 0.4bn), less krona, logistics, chemicals and wage headwinds (about SEK 0.3bn to 0.5bn), with Nordic wood flat as management guides. EPS arithmetic: each SEK 100m of EBITDA is about SEK 0.31 of EPS (22% tax, 248.7m shares, D&A about SEK 2.87bn, interest about SEK 0.3bn). My central EBITDA therefore implies FY2027 EPS of about SEK 4.1 to 4.8, against FactSet's 5.63. The range of 3.9bn to 5.3bn implies EPS of about SEK 2.3 to 6.7.

Data conflicts the Consumer must not merge.

  • Sack paper is 9% of sales in the product mix, but Jefferies cites a "20% group sales exposure". The 20% probably includes kraft and specialty paper (16%).
  • LPB is given as 21%, about 22% and 23% of sales.
  • North American utilisation is given as 68% (2024), 75% and over 90% (Jefferies, summer 2026).
  • The Evolution programme is SEK 1.4bn or SEK 1.3bn (cut on FX).
  • 2026 capex was guided at SEK 2.6bn and then SEK 2.4bn.
  • Net debt to EBITDA is 2.0x (company, Q3 2026) or 2.31x (FactSet, including leases).
  • The carbon effect is given as SEK 507m (2025 net sale of surplus allowances), SEK 141m (an EBIT headwind), SEK 137m (Q3) and SEK -57m (Q3 carbon tax). These are different measures.
  • The DNB Carnegie target is SEK 88 (22 October text) or SEK 82 (FactSet, dated 2 November).
  • The Risks report is dated August 2026 and predates the Q3 beat.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Billerud AB (BILL-SE), a Swedish producer of virgin-fibre packaging materials (liquid packaging board, cartonboard, sack and kraft paper, containerboard and fluting, market pulp) with two segments: Region Europe (six mills in Sweden and Finland) and Region North America (the Escanaba and Quinnesec mills in Michigan, making mainly coated graphic and label paper while converting part of their output to packaging board).

Request: T-P1

Peers: Stora Enso (the closest European rival in liquid packaging board and folding boxboard; running a EUR 500m to 700m cost programme while ramping the Oulu board line); Metsä Board (a non-integrated Nordic board and white kraftliner producer with a EUR 200m transformation programme and the Tako closure); Mayr-Melnhof Karton (a European cartonboard producer with the Fit-For-Future cost programme); Mondi (the European sack and kraft paper leader, facing Central European wood and energy inflation); SCA and Holmen (integrated Swedish forest-owning board and kraftliner producers, the cost benchmark); UPM-Kymmene (precedent for closing graphic paper capacity and running a Nordic mill system with low forest ownership); Sappi (North American graphic paper mills under secular decline, and the Somerset conversion to board); Smurfit Westrock and International Paper (North American and European capacity closures and their effect on unit costs). Inputs: What is the fixed and variable cost split of Billerud's European and North American mill systems? The company presented about 75% variable and 25% fixed for Europe, and about 56% variable and 44% fixed for North America (Capital Markets Day, 3 December 2024); is that still current? How did EBITDA and EBIT margins at Billerud (and at BillerudKorsnäs before 2022) and at the named peers move with delivery volumes in the 2008 to 2009, 2012 to 2013, 2019 to 2020 and 2023 downturns, and in the 2010, 2017 to 2018 and 2021 to 2022 upturns, with dated figures? How much of Billerud's SEK 1.5bn efficiency programme (closed at the end of 2024) and its SEK 800m programme (announced 18 September 2025: SEK 550m in 2026, a further SEK 250m in 2027, up to 650 positions) has reached reported EBITDA after wage, maintenance and other inflation, and how does that compare with what was promised and with peers' programmes? Which cost lines proved cuttable at Billerud and peers (personnel, administration, maintenance, logistics, chemicals procurement), and which did not (wood, energy, mill fixed costs without closure), and why? What do scheduled maintenance shutdowns cost by mill and by year, how often do they overrun (Quinnesec in Q2 2026, Escanaba in Q3 2026), and what is a normal annual level? How much EBITDA does each point of overall equipment effectiveness add, as the company and brokers state it, and what OEE gains have Billerud and peers actually achieved? How does unit cost change at the North American mills as graphic paper volumes fall and packaging grades (Tribute, Voyager) are qualified, and at what utilisation do those mills break even on each grade? What did closures of high-cost European machines (Metsä Board Tako, Stora Enso Skutskär line 3, Smurfit Westrock Birmingham, Reno de Medici Barcelona) do to the remaining system's margins, and what did they cost in cash? How does depreciation (SEK 2,868m in 2025, FactSet) compare with sustainable maintenance capex, and what is the scope and timing of the Gävle recovery boiler investment? Focus: How much of Billerud's EBITDA recovery from its 2026 trough comes from permanent fixed-cost reduction and how much from reversible items (maintenance timing, wood lag), and what margin a 1% to 3% European volume change produces given the fixed-cost base. Also, whether a European capacity closure is economically feasible for Billerud and what it would do to group margins.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Stora Enso (global leader in liquid packaging board and Billerud's main competitor for liquid packaging board contracts; adding folding boxboard volume from Oulu); Metsä Board (white kraftliner and folding boxboard; its US exports hit by tariffs and redirected to Europe); Mondi (price setter in European sack kraft paper, integrated into bags); SCA (virgin kraftliner, integrated with own forests); Holmen (Iggesund solid bleached board, value-over-volume pricing); Smurfit Westrock and SAICA (recycled containerboard price leaders, setting the recycled-versus-virgin gap); Nippon Paper's Dynawave mill (liquid packaging board supply shock in 2026); Sappi, Graphic Packaging and Clearwater Paper (US solid bleached board competitors in the grades Billerud is entering); Sylvamo and other North American uncoated and coated paper producers (graphic paper pricing behind tariffs); Tetra Pak, SIG and Elopak (the liquid packaging board customers, for the buyer's view); Palm and Eren Paper (private recycled containerboard entrants in the UK from 2027). Operating conditions: Rising Nordic wood costs with weak demand (2021 to mid-2025, after the Russian wood import ban); falling Nordic wood costs with storm-felled supply (late 2025 to 2026); European overcapacity in folding boxboard and cartonboard (the Oulu ramp-up and Chinese exports, 2025 to 2027); tightening virgin kraftliner and sack paper supply after North American closures (2025 to 2026); new recycled containerboard capacity entering Europe (2027); a strong Swedish krona against the euro and US dollar; US import tariffs on paper and board (from August 2025); high European gas prices raising recycled producers' costs (October 2026); consumer down-trading and slower plastic-to-paper substitution; liquid packaging board contract renewal rounds. Inputs: How are Billerud's multi-year liquid packaging board contracts structured: length, indexation to inflation or to cost indices, escalation clauses, renegotiation triggers and lags; how much of the price rise of about 8% to 9% achieved at the start of 2024 (Jefferies) has held, and how did prices behave when wood costs fell in 2026? In folding boxboard and cartonboard, how much of the 2026 wood cost relief has been given back to customers through lower prices, and how quickly? How have virgin kraftliner, white-top kraftliner, sack kraft and semichemical fluting prices moved against recycled testliner since 2021, and what happened to the virgin premium in earlier periods when recycled capacity was added? Who leads sack paper price changes (Mondi), and how have Billerud's sack prices tracked the quarterly increases (for example, EUR 50 a tonne into Q4 2026, Jefferies, 8 October 2026)? How have Billerud's North American graphic and label paper prices moved since the August 2025 tariffs, and what share of the tariff did importers pass on? How much of Billerud's revenue change in each quarter of 2025 and 2026 came from price, volume, mix and currency? What do customers, competitors and brokers now say about price increases planned for Q4 2026 and H1 2027 in each grade? How concentrated are Billerud's customers (the company states the five largest are about one-third of sales), and what are the switching costs and qualification times in each grade? Focus: Whether Billerud's 2026 price increases are real (above cost) or only cost-driven, and in which grades they will hold through 2027 as Nordic wood stabilises, recycled capacity arrives and competitors fill new machines. Above all, whether liquid packaging board and cartonboard prices will give back the 2026 wood cost relief.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: Billerud's earnings depend on a few interacting loops (wood cost relief given back through price, scarce-grade prices inviting recycled supply, low returns forcing capacity closures, tariffs driving domestic qualification), which decide whether the 2026 recovery compounds or reverses in 2027 and 2028. Peers: Stora Enso and Metsä Board (incentives to fill new or idle capacity, and the main candidates for price competition); SCA and Holmen (integrated forest owners whose forest profits move against their mill costs); Mondi (Central European wood exposure, the opposite of Billerud's); Smurfit Westrock and International Paper (North American closures that restored pricing); Palm and Eren Paper (private recycled entrants responding to virgin price premiums); Södra and Sveaskog (Swedish forest owners and wood sellers, for the supply-side response). Inputs: Start from these possible dynamics. (1) Balancing: lower Nordic wood costs leading to lower board prices in oversupplied grades, so the cost saving passes to customers. (2) Balancing: the rising premium of virgin kraftliner and sack over recycled grades attracting recycled capacity (King's Lynn from March 2027, Shotton in 2027) and substitution, which narrows the premium. (3) Balancing: storm-felled timber lowering Swedish stumpage prices in 2026, followed by reduced felling and higher prices once salvage ends; management expects average 2027 wood costs to be stable against 2026 (Q3 2026 earnings call, 22 October 2026). (4) Reinforcing for the industry: persistently low returns in European board leading to permanent closures, which lift operating rates and prices for the remaining mills (and what has stopped closures so far). (5) Reinforcing: US tariffs leading customers to prefer domestic supply, driving faster qualification of Billerud's North American packaging grades and higher mill utilisation. (6) Reinforcing: cost savings and OEE gains lowering unit costs, which allows volume to be won without price cuts. (7) Balancing: a stronger krona as Swedish exporters' earnings recover, eroding the SEK value of foreign revenue. (8) The carbon loop: fossil-free mills losing free emission allowances, and the possible re-entry to the EU emissions trading system. Focus: Which of these loops are operating now, with dated evidence, and which will dominate Billerud's European margin in 2027 and 2028. Above all, whether the wood-relief give-back loop and the recycled-supply loop will offset the scarce-grade price gains, and what would trigger European capacity closures.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: Billerud's earnings per share are highly sensitive to items below and around EBITDA (carbon, currency hedges, maintenance timing, depreciation, tax, capex and working capital), on which broker forecasts differ widely and the company gives only partial guidance. Peers: Stora Enso and Metsä Board (Nordic mills facing the same emission trading changes and krona exposure); Holmen and SCA (Swedish peers with similar tax, currency and carbon rules); Mondi (hedging policy contrast: does not hedge forecast sales); Mayr-Melnhof Karton (maintenance cost cycles and capex normalisation). Inputs: Carbon: what was the effect on Billerud's EBITDA of losing free emission allowances and paying Swedish fossil carbon tax in 2026, against the net sale of surplus allowances in 2025 (about SEK 507m, Jefferies), and what will re-entry to the EU ETS1 from 1 January 2027 (Swedish Ministry of Climate and Enterprise proposal, including any retroactive application to 2026) and ETS2 purchases from 2028 cost or return? Currency: given the hedge ratios and rates the company disclosed for the next 15 months (Q3 2026 interim report), and its sensitivity of about SEK 640m of EBITDA per 10% krona move before hedges (2025 annual report), what currency effect do brokers assume for 2027 and 2028, and what do currency forecasters expect for EUR/SEK and USD/SEK? Maintenance: what are the planned shutdown costs by mill for 2027 and 2028 against SEK 1,029m in the first nine months of 2026 (Q3 2026 interim report)? Wood: what Swedish and Finnish pulpwood and stumpage price paths do forest-owner associations, Skogsstyrelsen and brokers expect for 2027, and with what lag do they reach Billerud's costs? Depreciation, net interest, tax rate and share count: what do brokers assume for 2027 and 2028, and why do the named brokers' 2027 EPS forecasts range from SEK 4.24 (Jefferies, 22 October 2026) to SEK 6.61 (Nordea, 5 November 2026)? Capex: what is the size, timing and phasing of the Gävle recovery boiler decision (stated as above SEK 3bn, decision in 2027) and its effect on the dividend? Working capital: how much of the 2025 and 2026 cash conversion came from receivables reduction, factoring or supplier finance arrangements, and is it reversible? North American packaging: what volumes of Tribute and other packaging grades do brokers assume for 2027 and 2028 against the 300kt target for 2030? Focus: For 2027 and 2028, what each of carbon, currency, maintenance, wood, depreciation, interest, tax and capex is likely to contribute to the change in Billerud's EPS and free cash flow, and which of them explain the wide spread between brokers' forecasts.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 21 November 2026, 16:01 to 16:16 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Billerud (BILL-SE): answers to the second set of questions (financial forecasts), as at 21 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer

What this set does. It turns the first set's judgements into an operating and financial model for FY2026 to FY2030, with the next six quarters phased. The forecast inputs file holds the numbers; these answers hold the reasoning. All money is in SEK millions unless stated. EBITDA and EPS are on the brokers' adjusted basis (before items affecting comparability), which is the basis FactSet consensus uses. Per SEK 100m of EBITDA, EPS moves by about SEK 0.31 (22% tax, 248.7m shares). I agree with the first set on that figure.

My base case in one table (adjusted, SEKm, EPS in SEK).

Revenue 40,516 39,747 40,857 41,881 42,928 44,001

Consensus EBITDA (FactSet median) 3,457 4,923 5,258 6,082 (1 broker)
Consensus EPS (FactSet median) 1.32 5.63 6.51 9.10 (1 broker)

The four places where I differ from the first set, and why they matter.

  1. FY2027 EBITDA: SEK 4.5bn, at the bottom of the first set's SEK 4.5bn to 4.7bn. The first set built FY2027 from the H2 2026 run-rate "with Nordic wood flat as management guides". But the H2 2026 run-rate already contains the lowest wood cost of the cycle, and management guides the 2027 average to be stable against the 2026 average. Read literally, that guidance means wood costs in H2 2027 are higher than in H2 2026 by about as much as H1 2027 is lower than H1 2026. Applying the run-rate and the flat-average guidance together counts the wood relief twice, by up to about SEK 0.4bn to 0.6bn. I take a middle reading (about +SEK 0.2bn of wood benefit in 2027 against 2026), because the guidance wording is ambiguous and Swedish salvage supply runs into early 2027.
  2. Net financial items: about SEK 0.2bn a year, not SEK 0.3bn. Jefferies models SEK 200m for 2027 and DNB Carnegie SEK 220m; FactSet consensus implies about SEK 144m for 2026. The first set used SEK 0.3bn. The difference is worth about SEK 0.3 of EPS and partly offsets point 1.
  3. Liquid packaging board (LPB) price is a faster-moving variable than the first set allowed. The chief executive said in January 2026 that 100% of LPB volume now sits on annual price cycles (AlphaSense pricing report, citing the Q4 2025 call). The first set treated LPB as protected by multi-year contracts. The likely reconciliation is multi-year volume frameworks with annual price setting: volume is sticky, price is not. That moves the LPB give-back risk into the January 2027 round.
  4. FY2026 dividend: SEK 1.50, below the first set's SEK 1.75 to 2.00 and below consensus SEK 2.00. Reported net profit for 2026 will be close to zero (items affecting comparability of about SEK 0.3bn in the first nine months), the Gävle boiler decision is in 2027, and the board cut the 2025 dividend more than the market expected.

The single most useful forecast signature for the third and fourth sets. EBITDA growth in 2027 is almost entirely in the first-half comparisons. My quarterly path is Q1 2027 SEK 1.16bn (+122% year on year), Q2 SEK 1.09bn (+64%), Q3 SEK 1.12bn (+8%), Q4 SEK 1.13bn (-2%). H2 2026 already contains the wood relief and the H2 2026 price rises. H2 2027 then meets three balancing forces on dated clocks: the end of Swedish storm-salvage supply, UK recycled containerboard capacity from March 2027, and euro hedges falling from 80% (Q1 2027) to 38% (Q2 2027). A flat-to-down Q4 2027 against Q4 2026 is the expected path, not a miss, unless the Consumer believes wood stays at today's spot level.
