Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

Billerud reports Region Europe (six mills in Sweden and Finland), Region North America (Escanaba and Quinnesec, Michigan) and Other (group functions, the energy and wood-trading activities, and hedging). Region-level revenue for FY2026 onward is my estimate; "Other" EBITDA is derived as group adjusted EBITDA less the two regions (about SEK -174m in Q3 2026).

Segment (SEKm) FY2025A FY2026E FY2027E FY2028E FY2029E FY2030E
Region Europe revenue 25,640 about 24,600 about 25,700 about 26,500 about 27,300 about 28,100
Region Europe EBITDA (margin) about 2,300 (about 9%) about 2,250 (about 9%) about 3,100 (about 12%) about 3,250 (about 12.3%) about 3,450 (about 12.6%) about 3,650 (about 13%)
Region North America revenue 11,783 about 12,050 about 12,100 about 12,300 about 12,500 about 12,700
Region North America EBITDA (margin) 2,307 (19.6%) about 1,800 (about 15%) about 2,000 (about 16.5%) about 2,100 (about 17%) about 2,200 (about 17.5%) about 2,300 (about 18%)
Other and hedging, revenue 3,065 about 3,100 about 3,050 about 3,100 about 3,150 about 3,200
Other, EBITDA about -500 about -670 about -600 about -650 about -670 about -670
Group adjusted EBITDA 4,098 3,378 4,494 4,691 4,980 5,280
  • North America: 2026 actual quarters were SEK 2,748m / 453m (16%), 3,103m / 399m (13%) and 3,174m / 472m (15%); Q4 2026 about 3,050m / 470m. 2027 benefits from no Quinnesec shutdown (biennial; next in 2028), no repeat of the 2026 overruns, packaging volume of about 80kt (Q3 2026 run-rate 44kt) and tariff-supported graphic prices, against a 5% graphic volume decline and a weaker dollar. The target is SEK 2.0bn to 2.5bn.
  • Europe: Q3 2026 SEK 734m (about 12%), against an estimated SEK 245m in Q1 and SEK 425m in Q2. 2027 carries the full SEK 800m programme (mostly European), lagged wood relief in H1, kraftliner, sack and fluting price, and partial LPB and cartonboard give-back. The target of SEK 4.3bn to 4.8bn is not reached in the forecast without European capacity closures.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Revenue. Group deliveries about 3.5Mt in 2026 (Q3 903kt), +1.5% in 2027, +2.0% in 2028, +1.5% a year after. Group price about +2.0% in 2027 (index, 2026 average = 100: Q1 2027 102, Q2 102.5, Q3 102.5, Q4 102; after about SEK 0.1bn of LPB and cartonboard give-back in the January 2027 rounds) and about +1% a year from 2028 (cost pass-through as Nordic wood rises). Krona about 2% to 3% stronger on average in 2027, reducing revenue by about 1%. North American packaging volume about 40kt (2026), 80kt (2027), 130kt (2028), 190kt (2029), 250kt (2030); graphic paper -5% a year.
  • FY2027 EBITDA bridge (SEKm): FY2026 3,378; savings +250; maintenance +100 (about SEK 1.11bn in 2026 to about SEK 1.01bn in 2027); North American non-recurrence +100; OEE +100; wood +200 (middle reading of management's "stable on average against 2026"); price +750; non-wood and wage inflation -425; krona net of hedges -150; carbon +100 (ETS1 re-entry ends the Swedish fossil carbon tax); volume +100; FY2027 about 4,500.
  • FY2028: wood about +3% (-SEK 0.25bn), Quinnesec shutdown returns (-SEK 0.1bn), ETS2 purchases (-SEK 0.05bn), krona (-SEK 0.05bn), offset by volume and mix (+SEK 0.3bn), North American ramp (+SEK 0.1bn), OEE (+SEK 0.1bn) and wood pass-through in price (+SEK 0.15bn): EBITDA about SEK 4.7bn.
  • FY2029 to FY2030: neutral cycle; margin to 11.6% and 12.0% on the North American ramp, OEE and volume. No European closure is included.
  • Quarterly phasing. Q4 2026: maintenance SEK 80m (Frövi) against SEK 403m in Q3, price +1%, input costs -SEK 60m, seasonal European volume dip; EBITDA about SEK 1,160m. Q1 2027: full savings run-rate (SEK 200m a quarter), lowest lagged wood, euro flows 80% hedged at SEK 10.97, low maintenance, winter energy; about SEK 1,165m. Q2 2027: European maintenance season (about SEK 380m), hedges at 38%; about SEK 1,090m. Q3 2027: Escanaba and European shutdowns (about SEK 380m), wood starting to rise after salvage ends, UK recycled capacity ramping; about SEK 1,120m. Q4 2027: low maintenance but wood higher and hedges rolled off; about SEK 1,135m, slightly below Q4 2026. Q1 2028: wood higher than in Q1 2027; about SEK 1,130m. (The quarters sum to about SEK 4,504m against SEK 4,494m for the year, a rounding difference.)
  • Below EBITDA. D&A flat at about SEK 2.87bn to 2.88bn (capex below depreciation) until Evolution and then Gävle assets enter service (2029 to 2030). Net financial items about SEK 0.15bn to 0.25bn on net debt of SEK 5bn to 6bn (Jefferies SEK 200m and DNB Carnegie SEK 220m for 2027). Tax 22% (Swedish 20.6%, US about 25%). No dilution, no buybacks.
  • Dividend. Declared for the fiscal year and paid the following spring. FY2026 SEK 1.50 (reported 2026 profit near zero after about SEK 0.3bn of items affecting comparability; Gävle pending; range SEK 1.00 to 2.00); thereafter about 50% of adjusted EPS.
  • Capex. SEK 2.4bn in 2026 (guided; SEK 1,566m spent in the first nine months) and SEK 2.6bn in 2027 (guided: SEK 2.1bn base plus SEK 0.5bn strategic, of which SEK 460m Evolution). From 2028 a base of about SEK 2.2bn to 2.3bn plus the Gävle recovery boiler (above SEK 3bn; decision in 2027; production around 2030), phased about SEK 0.8bn (2028), 1.4bn (2029) and 0.9bn (2030).
  • Working capital. 13.6% of revenue at end-2025 and 13.2% at Q3 2026 (FactSet definition: receivables plus inventories less payables). A seasonal and price-driven year-end 2026 of 13.0%, then a rebuild to 13.3% as prices and volumes rise; the 2025 receivables release (SEK 677m) and the fall in supplier finance (SEK 711m to SEK 327m) do not repeat.
  • Leases. About SEK 150m a year (right-of-use assets about SEK 0.3bn; FactSet debt includes leases).

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

Billerud gives no annual revenue or EBITDA guidance. Its guided blocks, and my treatment:

  • Q4 2026: maintenance SEK 80m (Frövi) — used; input costs about -SEK 60m (two-thirds logistics) — used; price about +1% in both regions — used; wood flat — used.
  • 2027: wood costs "on average stable compared with 2026 despite potential pressure after storm felling ceases" — I use a middle reading (+SEK 0.2bn of benefit, about -2% on average), between a literal average-on-average reading (0) and a "flat from the Q4 2026 exit rate" reading (+SEK 0.4bn to 0.6bn); positive pricing into H1 2027 — used (+1% a quarter in Q4 2026 and Q1 2027, then flat); savings of a further SEK 250m to reach SEK 800m — used in full; capex SEK 2.6bn — used.
  • Capex 2026 SEK 2.4bn (cut from SEK 2.6bn) — used; base capex about SEK 2.2bn after 2027 — used, plus Gävle.
  • Currency: hedge cover and rates for 15 months (euro 80% in Q1 2027 falling to 38% in Q2 2027, at about SEK 10.97; US dollar about 35% to 44% at about SEK 9.33 to 9.41) — used to phase the krona effect into H2 2027.
  • Carbon: Swedish proposal to re-enter ETS1 from 1 January 2027 (retroactivity not enacted); ETS2 purchases from 2028 — used, with small net effects (+SEK 0.1bn in 2027, -SEK 0.05bn from 2028).
  • Mid-term targets "over a business cycle": EBITDA above SEK 6bn (Europe SEK 4.3bn to 4.8bn, North America SEK 2.0bn to 2.5bn), EBITDA margin above 15%, ROCE above 11%, net debt to EBITDA below 2.5x, cash conversion above 80%, dividend at least 50% of net profit. My forecasts meet the leverage, cash conversion and North American targets, and do not meet the EBITDA, margin, European or ROCE targets in any year to 2030.

Against consensus (FactSet median): my FY2027 EBITDA SEK 4,494m is 9% below SEK 4,923m and close to Jefferies (SEK 4,532m) and Pareto (SEK 4,519m); my FY2027 EPS SEK 4.40 is 22% below SEK 5.63 and between Jefferies (SEK 4.24) and Pareto (SEK 4.49). FY2028 EBITDA SEK 4,691m is 11% below SEK 5,258m.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • EBITDA. Consensus is on the brokers' adjusted basis, which excludes items affecting comparability (restructuring, write-offs, divestment gains). My EBITDA margin inputs are on that adjusted basis. FactSet's history line is "operating" EBITDA, which includes some of these items: FY2025 FactSet operating EBITDA SEK 3,922m against SEK 4,098m on the brokers' basis (+4.5%); in the first nine months of 2026 FactSet operating EBITDA SEK 1,914m (349, 584, 981) against company adjusted EBITDA SEK 2,218m (525, 661, 1,032), a gap of SEK 304m. I assume items affecting comparability of about SEK -350m for FY2026, about SEK -100m for FY2027 and about SEK -50m a year after; they are excluded from the inputs and from my EPS, and included in my free cash flow only where they are cash.
  • Carbon. Net sales of surplus emission allowances (about SEK 507m in FY2025, SEK 471m in FY2024) sat inside adjusted EBITDA; they do not return, so FY2024 and FY2025 adjusted bases are not like-for-like with FY2026 onward.
  • Hedging results run through revenue ("Currency hedging etc." segment line; SEK -28m in FY2025).
  • EPS. My cross-check EPS is adjusted (brokers' basis): adjusted EBITDA less D&A less net financial items, taxed at 22%, over 248.7m shares. Reported basic EPS will be lower by the after-tax items affecting comparability (FY2026 reported EPS close to zero). Do not compare with FactSet's "diluted before unusual items" (FY2025 SEK 4.65) or the Viewforth definition (FY2025 SEK 5.42).
  • Net debt and leverage. The company reports net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 2.0x at Q3 2026 excluding leases; FactSet shows SEK 6,020m of net debt including leases and a trailing 2.31x on its own EBITDA. My leverage figures use FactSet's basis on adjusted EBITDA.
  • Free cash flow. FactSet's history defines free cash flow as operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex (not deducting leases). My cross-check deducts lease payments as well; the difference is about SEK 150m a year.
  • Dividends. FactSet's DPS line is dated by payment period in some years; my DPS is the dividend declared for each fiscal year (for example, SEK 1.50 declared for FY2026 and paid in 2027).

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

Earnings per share 1.09 4.40 5.12 5.86 6.52

EPS is adjusted (brokers' basis). Free cash flow is adjusted EBITDA less cash items affecting comparability, capex, net interest, tax paid, lease payments and the change in working capital; it is before dividends (SEK 497m paid in 2026, then about SEK 373m, 535m, 634m and 721m).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Billerud AB (BILL-SE), a Swedish producer of virgin-fibre paper and packaging materials (liquid packaging board, cartonboard, sack and kraft paper, containerboard and fluting, market pulp), reporting two regions: Region Europe (six mills in Sweden and Finland) and Region North America (the Escanaba and Quinnesec mills in Michigan, mainly coated graphic and label paper, part converting to packaging board under the Evolution programme), plus Other.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Stora Enso (largest liquid packaging board rival; ran a large cost programme while ramping the Oulu board line); Metsä Board (non-integrated Nordic board producer with a EUR 200m transformation programme and the Tako closure); Mayr-Melnhof Karton (European cartonboard producer with the Fit-For-Future cost programme); Mondi (European sack and kraft paper leader); SCA and Holmen (integrated Swedish forest-owning producers, the cost benchmark); UPM-Kymmene (precedent for closing Nordic paper capacity and running mills with limited forest ownership); Sappi and Sylvamo (North American paper producers converting or defending graphic paper capacity); International Paper and Smurfit Westrock (precedents for capacity closures restoring margins). Operating conditions: falling Nordic wood costs after storm-felled supply (late 2025 to 2026) followed by the end of salvage felling (2027); a strong Swedish krona against the euro and the US dollar; European oversupply in folding boxboard and cartonboard; scarce virgin kraftliner and sack paper after North American closures; new recycled containerboard capacity in the UK from 2027; US import tariffs on paper and board; loss of free emission allowances for fossil-free Swedish mills. Inputs: Has Billerud, or BillerudKorsnäs before it, ever sustained an EBITDA margin gain for two consecutive years without a falling wood price or a rising board price, and what made the difference in those years? How much of the SEK 1.5bn efficiency programme (closed end-2024) and the SEK 800m programme (announced 18 September 2025; SEK 550m in 2026, full run-rate from 2027) is visible in reported EBITDA after wage, logistics and chemical inflation, and how does that compare with the net EBITDA effect of Stora Enso's, Metsä Board's and Mayr-Melnhof Karton's programmes? What overall equipment effectiveness gains have Billerud and peers actually reported, against the company's statement that one point is worth about SEK 100m of EBITDA? How often have Billerud's North American maintenance shutdowns and start-ups run over budget since 2022 (for example Quinnesec in Q2 2026, SEK 50m over plan), and what does a normal year cost? How have non-integrated Nordic producers' margins behaved in the 12 to 24 months after a storm-driven wood price fall (for example after Storm Gudrun in 2005 and the 2013 to 2014 storms)? Has any non-integrated producer converted graphic paper machines to packaging board at low capital cost and reached its volume target on time, and how long did it take (Billerud targets 300kt of North American packaging by 2030, against 11kt in Q3 2026)? Focus: Whether the earnings recovery now under way rests on strengths Billerud has demonstrated (cost programmes, capital discipline, qualified virgin-fibre grades) or on external conditions it has never been able to hold through a cycle (wood, price, currency). Above all, what the record says about retaining a margin gain once the Nordic wood price stops falling.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Stora Enso and Metsä Board (same Nordic wood, krona and emissions exposures, with different integration); SCA and Holmen (integrated forest owners whose profits move with the same wood price in the opposite direction); Mondi (Central European wood exposure, unhedged currency policy); Mayr-Melnhof Karton (maintenance and cartonboard pricing cycles); Smurfit Westrock and SAICA (set recycled containerboard prices and so the recycled-versus-virgin gap); Elopak, SIG and Tetra Pak (liquid packaging board buyers). Operating conditions: the 2027 annual liquid packaging board price round; Swedish pulpwood prices after salvage felling ends; UK recycled containerboard start-ups (Palm King's Lynn from March 2027, Eren Shotton in 2027); the roll-off of currency hedges during 2027; Swedish emissions trading re-entry and ETS2 from 2028; European construction demand for sack paper; US tariff changes. Inputs: Which few variables explain most of the year-to-year change in Billerud's EBITDA since 2019 (Nordic wood price, selling price by grade, the krona, maintenance, volume, emission allowance income), and how large was each in each year? How wide has the range between the highest and lowest broker forecast for Billerud's next-year EBITDA been at this point in past years, and how did outturns compare with the range? Named brokers' FY2027 EPS currently ranges from SEK 4.24 (Jefferies, 22 October 2026) to SEK 6.61 (Nordea, 5 November 2026): which assumptions (EBITDA, depreciation, net financial items, tax) explain the spread? How often have Billerud's quarterly results missed or beaten consensus by more than 10% since 2021, in which quarters of the year, and why? How did the virgin kraftliner premium over recycled testliner behave in past periods when large recycled machines started in Europe? How do Billerud's euro and US dollar hedge ratios and rates (Q3 2026 interim report) and currency forecasters' EUR/SEK and USD/SEK paths for 2027 translate into a range of currency effects? What is the range of outcomes for the 2027 liquid packaging board price, given annual price setting (chief executive, Q4 2025 call) and the Nippon Paper Dynawave outage? Focus: How concentrated Billerud's 2027 and 2028 outcomes are on a small number of external variables, and how wide and in which direction the plausible range for the next 18 months is skewed. Above all, whether the timing of those variables makes the second half of 2027 materially less predictable than the first half.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Stora Enso, Metsä Board, Mondi, SCA, Holmen and Mayr-Melnhof Karton (European peers reporting in the same weeks, with read-across on wood, price and currency); Smurfit Westrock and International Paper (containerboard price and closure read-across); Sappi, Sylvamo and Graphic Packaging (North American paper and board read-across); Elopak and SIG (liquid packaging board pricing from the buyer side). Operating conditions: the Q4 2026 report on 2 February 2027 and the 2027 quarterly reports; the 2027 Nordic wood price lists; the January 2027 liquid packaging board price round; sequential price increases in kraftliner, sack and fluting into H1 2027; UK recycled capacity from March 2027; the krona; Swedish emissions trading legislation; the Gävle recovery boiler investment decision in 2027. Inputs: Billerud gives no annual earnings guidance; what has it guided for Q4 2026 (maintenance SEK 80m at Frövi, input costs about SEK 60m higher, about 1% price increases in both regions, flat wood) and for 2027 (wood costs stable on average against 2026, positive pricing into H1 2027, a further SEK 250m of savings, capex SEK 2.6bn), and how have such quarterly blocks compared with outcomes in 2024 to 2026? Where does FactSet consensus (FY2027 EBITDA SEK 4,923m median, 19 November 2026) sit against each named broker and against the run-rate implied by the latest quarters? How should management's 2027 wood guidance be read: stable against the 2026 average, or stable from current levels, and what do brokers assume? What did peers' Q3 2026 reports and commentary say about Q4 2026 and 2027 price, volume, wood and currency, and what does that imply for Billerud? What would have to happen for the dividend for 2026 to be above or below FactSet's SEK 2.00, given the policy of at least 50% of net profit and the 2025 cut to SEK 2.00 from SEK 3.50? How have estimate revisions behaved after Billerud's Q4 and Q1 reports in past years? What would move the mid-term target of above SEK 6bn of EBITDA over a cycle? Focus: Whether FY2027 and FY2028 consensus is more likely to be upgraded, held or cut over the next four reports, and what each outcome needs. Above all, how much of the expected 2027 improvement depends on wood and price conditions persisting into the second half of 2027.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Stora Enso (forest listing and Oulu narrative); SCA (the other Swedish fresh-fibre mill, described by Jefferies as one of the most levered names to European kraftliner prices); Holmen (forest-owner narrative); Mondi (the losing side of the same price rise, on Central European wood and energy); Metsä Board (distressed peer, US tariff exposure); Smurfit Westrock (recycled containerboard and gas costs); Elopak and SIG (liquid packaging board buyers). Operating conditions: the narrative that Billerud is the purest way to own scarce fresh-fibre kraft paper and lower Swedish wood; the share price up about 43% in six months; two of five named brokers at Hold (Jefferies, 29 September 2026; DNB Carnegie, 1 October 2026); Stora Enso's extraordinary general meeting on 15 December 2026; UK recycled start-ups in 2027; a risk-off market regime. Inputs: the end of Swedish storm-salvage wood and the meaning of management's 2027 wood guidance; the move of liquid packaging board to annual price setting and the 2027 round; the timing of the UK recycled capacity and its effect on the virgin premium; the roll-off of Billerud's currency hedges during 2027 and Riksbank-driven krona strength; the loss of free emission allowances, Swedish ETS1 re-entry and ETS2; the Gävle recovery boiler decision and its effect on dividends and buybacks; a possible European capacity closure by Billerud (Karlsborg and Pietarsaari have been named by Jefferies as candidates) or by peers; North American Evolution volumes (Tribute and Voyager) against the 300kt target and US solid bleached board oversupply; the gap between named-broker forecasts and FactSet consensus; the difference between FY2027 EPS revisions and EBITDA and sales revisions. Focus: Which of these dynamics investors and analysts already discuss and which they do not, especially the timing of the end of salvage wood, the UK recycled start-ups and the hedge roll-off during 2027, and how the wood guidance is read. Above all, whether the market narrative treats 2027 as a year of continued improvement or as a year of holding the 2026 gains.

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 23 November 2026, 23:26 to 23:57 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Billerud (BILL-SE): answers to the third and fourth sets of questions, as at 23 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer

What these answers do. The third set tests how predictable the forecasts are over the next 18 months (six reports: Q4 2026 on 2 February 2027, then Q1 2027 to Q1 2028). The fourth set judges how far our view is already reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the market narrative. Money is in SEK millions unless stated. EBITDA and EPS are on the brokers' adjusted basis. The share price used is SEK 89.15 (19 November 2026).

The forecasts I test (the model's forecasts of record, which I leave unchanged). EBITDA: FY2026 3,378; FY2027 4,494 (margin 11.0%); FY2028 4,691. EPS: 1.19 (model) or 1.09 (cross-check); 4.40; 5.11. FactSet consensus medians: EBITDA 3,457; 4,923; 5,258. EPS 1.32; 5.63; 6.51.

Six findings that change how the earlier sets should be read. Each is developed in the answer named.

  1. There is no currency "hedge cliff" in 2027. The earlier sets read the hedge table as protection that falls away in mid-2027. It is a rolling 15-month programme, so every quarter is hedged progressively as it approaches. The krona remains a large variable, but it is not a dated second-half 2027 event (D-01).
  2. Carbon is neutral in 2027, not a gain of SEK 100m. The chief financial officer said so on the Q3 2026 call. Re-entry to the EU scheme is proposed from 1 January 2027 without retroactive effect (D-01).
  3. The 2027 liquid packaging board (LPB) price round leans towards flat, not down. This offsets finding 2 (D-01).
  4. Our FY2027 EBITDA is the second-half 2026 run-rate plus about 3%. Consensus needs about 12% more than that run-rate. The disagreement with consensus is therefore not about the recovery, which has already happened. It is about a second step of roughly SEK 0.5bn (D-01, I-02).
  5. The centre of street expectations for FY2027 EBITDA is SEK 4.8bn to 5.0bn, not the SEK 4.65bn the first set proposed. FactSet's mean (4,995) cannot be reproduced from the five named brokers (mean 4,768), so brokers we cannot see sit well above them (I-02).
  6. The model pays each year's dividend one year early. Its 18-month return of +9.0% is overstated by about 2.4 points for that reason (D-01).

I change no forecast input. My amendments to the FY2027 bridge offset each other, and two independent rebuilds of the year give SEK 4.45bn and about SEK 4.6bn against the model's SEK 4.49bn. The two differences that the model summary flags between the Analyst's cross-check and the model are explained in D-01. Neither shows a wrong input.

Co-movement of BILL-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.
